#2856. Drake Maye vs. Jordan Love: Which is the better quarterback?

We here at Acme Packing Company are careful not to judge quarterbacks on raw counting statistics unless they suit our needs. For instance, we love Brett Favre's longevity stats, yards, and touchdowns, but when it comes to interceptions, for all QBs with at least 2500 attempts, he's 75th (out of 147). Not too shabby! But usually, especially lately, counting stats have worked against us because the Packers run at SUCH a slow pace that Jordan Love really can't rack up any significant yardage or touchdown totals. And so, typically we rely on things like EPA (Expected Points Added) and CPOE (Completion Percentage Over Expected), which makes us feel all warm and fuzzy.

But fast forward to THIS season and suddenly Jordan Love LEADS THE LEAGUE IN PASSING YARDS. And also attempts, which is not as good, but still noteworthy. This is also new and fascinating, because we've all been asking to let Jordan cook for quite a while, but with all that volume, plus a trash running game (and some other factors, which we will get to), he hasn't really been himself from an efficiency standpoint. He's still more efficient than an average quarterback with .15 EPA per play (down significantly from .289 last year), and so the offense still works, but it's a big drop. On that point, the only quarterback who had a better EPA per play last season was this week's opponent, Drake Maye (.322).

Maye is in his third season, and unquestionably great, but just how great is still a bit of a mystery. Last year he was probably the best quarterback in football, leading the way in EPA per play and the NextGenStats' version of CPOE, which is widely regarded as the most accurate version. But like Love, Maye didn't pass that much, finishing 12th in attempts with 492 (Love was 19th with 439, but he also missed two games. They were very close on a per-game basis).

Maye's numbers are still good this year as he's third among active starters in EPA per play (.279, down almost .05 from last year), but his volume has ALSO fallen as he ranks 17th in pass attempts behind players like Geno Smith and CJ Stroud. His CPOE has also decreased by over 50%, and he ranks seventh instead of first. Maye is, I think, having an objectively better season than Love in all the ways that matter from an individual player perspective. However, Love has almost certainly been the more valuable player, because when you are adding value to a football team, volume absolutely matters.

One of the big reasons the Patriots have thrown fewer passes than the Packers is because (how best to put this), the Patriots' running game doesn't suck as much as Green Bay's. None of this is hyperbole, and it's important to understand that both running games do in fact suck. The Pats as a unit have run 240 times for a 4.1 YPC average, however, that average is dragged up by Drake Maye scrambles. The top two Patriots running backs, TreVeyon Henderson and Rhamondre Stephenson, both average under four yards per carry, and have combined for 148 carries. The Packers as a unit average a ridiculously terrible 2.9 yards per carry, and as a result, they've only run the ball 170 times. The Patriots are definitely better, and they have definitely been better running AND passing without question. Their offense, by any account, should be MUCH more effective; however, in the aggregate, the Patriots only average 1.6 more points per game than Green Bay (please keep Trey Smack in mind while reading this) simply because the Packers pass more.

When it's all said and done, it seems like Maye is still the clear winner here, and I won't argue with anyone who makes this claim. He's an outstanding young player. But even so, it's not quite so cut and dried. Both quarterbacks have suffered this season compared to last season based on factors outside of their control. Love has suffered from a terrible offensive line and running game. Maye has lost Romeo Doubs and AJ Brown for extended periods, and his protection has struggled as well. And for Love, there is the added issue of drops.

The Packers have an insane number of drops. Matthew Golden leads the league in drops with 9, and overall, the Packers lead the league with a drop rate of a robust 7% of targets. Those are balls that Jordan Love has put on target (often for what should be big gains) and just don't happen. And on the other side, Patriots pass catchers have been some of the most reliable in the league, dropping just 2.5% of targets.

Love's efficiency numbers would be substantially higher with an average number of drops instead of whatever this is. Would it be enough to catch Maye? Ehhh, maybe not, but given how often the Packers are throwing the ball, it would certainly be enough to push the Packers' offense, in the aggregate, past the Pats.

In the end, it doesn't really matter if Maye is better or if Love is better. I think the real lesson is that quarterbacks really are impacted significantly by their surrounding cast, their volume, and their coaching, and that we can never really truly remove all of that context. Secondarily, it's important to account for that context when making predictions! I'm not sure that Matthew Golden is going to start catching everything, and I'm worried that Josh Jacobs' return is going to lead to more pointless running, but there is a path to victory here for Green Bay. Hopefully they take it.

#2851. Unfair NIL: Millionaire Quarterbacks, Broke Linemen

The numbers are finally in, and they tell a story that leaves many fans scratching their heads. A new database released on Friday by the College Sports Commission reveals just how wide the gap is between star quarterbacks and the players in the trenches. According to Fox Sports, the market is bearing a heavy load for some while others fly blind. John Bramlette, the head of operations for the commission, admitted that "Everyone to some degree or another has probably been flying blind as far as what the market is actually bearing." This lack of clarity has left schools and athletes guessing for far too long.

Let's be honest about the disparity. The data shows that the top 10 percent of paid Power Four quarterbacks are receiving $1.611 million in revenue share alone, with combined NIL and revenue-share totals climbing higher at the extreme end. That official College Sports Commission figure lines up with earlier summer estimates from Opendorse and others that already put elite quarterbacks well into seven figures.

Meanwhile, the median pay for players in other spots, like the offensive line or defensive back, is a fraction of that. It is a system where the face of the program gets the big checks, but the guys blocking for him are often just scraping by. This is about football and who gets valued in the eyes of the market.

The report sorted the money by position and conference, giving us a clear look at the divide. The revenue sharing for the top tier hit $662,000, making the gap between Power Four and non-Power Four schools in men's basketball dramatic regarding high-end spending. Football follows a similar pattern, but the stakes for the players at the bottom are much higher. The data might not capture every single dollar, but it reflects the reality that many players have tried to report on in less official ways.

#2849. Preview: Klay returns to Chase Center with new look Heat

It's a great time for sports fans to be in the Bay Area, Dub Nation! The Valkyries won their first WNBA title on Halloween night at Chase Center and paraded the trophy down the Embarcadero yesterday. Their older siblings the Golden State Warriors have won five straight. And tonight, the building that just hosted a championship celebration hosts the Miami Heat and a quasi homecoming for a guy who earned a few of those parades himself. Wassup, Klay Thompson!

The Warriors remember the last time Miami came to Chase. Jimmy Butler went down with a torn ACL that night, and he's still out tonight, along with Kristaps Porzingis, Moses Moody and Gui Santos.

Yesterday while San Francisco was sweeping violet confetti off the Embarcadero, Klay Thompson was in Inglewood remembering exactly who he is. He dropped 30 on 8-of-17 from deep to cap a 20-point comeback over the Clippers. Down two in overtime, Giannis grabbed a defensive rebound with 3.8 seconds left, Miami called timeout, and on the ensuing inbound Giannis found Klay open in the left corner off a Bam screen for the walk-off three. Per a stat making the rounds, it was the first game-winning three of Klay's career in the final five seconds of the fourth quarter or OT, regular season and playoffs. Klay's got four rings, 37 points in a quarter, Game 6 in OKC, and yet the walk-off three was the one trinket missing from the shelf.

The Warriors he's coming back to look nothing like the team that started 0-3. They've since beaten the Nuggets, Timberwolves, Lakers, Clippers and Suns, including the Clippers win with both Steph and Draymond sitting out, and Monday's win over Phoenix came after trailing by 17.

The South Beach super team rolls out Davion Mitchell, Thompson, Andrew Wiggins, Giannis Antetokounmpo and Bam Adebayo. Wiggins also won a title with the Warriors back in 2022 and got into the parade action; he joins Klay on the walk to the familiar arena.

Through eight games, Miami ranks dead last in the league in opponent threes made (17.1 per game) and opponent three-point percentage (40.5 percent), which is an engraved invitation for a Steph Curry who's averaging 30 a night. The flip side is a Golden State frontcourt that starts Draymond at center with rookie Yaxel Lendeborg at forward, staring down a two-time MVP and a Bam who just set season highs with 35 points and 10 rebounds. Miami's been getting nearly 20 points a night at the free throw line, so Lendeborg has to wall up Giannis without fouling. When the Warriors shoot, he has to find Giannis and put a body on him before the Greek Freak can turn a long rebound into a one-man fast break. Then there's the Steve Kerr and Erik Spoelstra subplot, two guys who drew up gold-medal sets on the same whiteboard in Paris now trying to burn each other's playbooks.

The schedule does hand Golden State an edge. The Warriors had two days off, while the Heat are on the second night of a back-to-back and their third game in four nights, and Klay's status is still uncertain. If he plays, Chase will rain love on him during introductions, and then 18,000 people will spend the next two hours praying he left his new walk-off habit in Inglewood.

#2845. NFL Thursday Night Football preview: Ravens to start rookie Joe Fagnano at QB vs. Jaguars

The Baltimore Ravens had hope before kickoff last Sunday. Ahead of their kickoff on Thursday, most of that hope has vanished with two months left in the season.

The Ravens suffered not one but two quarterback injuries on Sunday. Lamar Jackson dislocated his right thumb on his first play back from an ankle injury, then Tyler Huntley injured his toe. Both players are on injured reserve.

Before last Sunday, you would have had to have watched a lot of preseason football to know Joe Fagnano was the Ravens' third-string quarterback. The undrafted rookie is now elevated to QB1 for their game on Thursday night against a very good Jacksonville Jaguars team. There is help on the way next week, as the Ravens reportedly were finalizing a deal for Cardinals quarterback Jacoby Brissett, but Fagnano will get at least one start.

Fagnano started his college career as a walk-on at Maine. He went to UConn after that and had an impressive 28-to-1 touchdown-to-interception ratio for the Huskies. He still went undrafted and signed with the Ravens with no money guaranteed. In his first NFL action last week, he completed 10-of-21 passes for 91 yards. He will benefit from getting starter's reps in practice, even though it's a short week. It's still a very tough assignment.

Baltimore Ravens quarterback Joe Fagnano made his NFL debut last week against Buffalo.

The Jaguars are 5-2 and lead the NFL with a +85 point differential. They're up to No. 2 in DVOA behind the 49ers and are the only team that ranks in the top-five of offense, defense and special teams in DVOA. Three of their five wins have come by 24 or more points. It looks like they'll run away with the AFC South.

The Ravens are just trying to stay alive in case Jackson can make a return sometime late in the season. They have to wait one more week for Brissett to be the bridge quarterback to that. This week, they're relying on an undrafted rookie to help pull off an improbable win.

It's reasonable to say the Ravens. If they win they'll be 5-4 with Jacoby Brissett coming on board. The season wouldn't technically be over at that point. However, Baltimore's chances of staying afloat over the second half of the season and also having Lamar Jackson return are slim. When you lose a two-time MVP quarterback to injured reserve, there's not much you can do about it. The Jaguars are cruising along at 5-2 and look like a legitimate Super Bowl contender. They might not be the more desperate team, but they're the one with a bright future this season. They need it more just because a loss to a reeling team with an undrafted rookie quarterback would poor cold water on the idea that the Jaguars can win a championship.

If the Ravens can pull off an upset, it will be because the defense plays great and the offense carries its quarterback. That formula puts a lot of pressure on Henry. It won't be easy against a Jaguars team that will likely come in not respecting the Ravens' passing game with a rookie making his first start. The Jaguars also allow the fewest rushing yards and rushing yards per carry in the NFL. That did come with defensive tackle DaVon Hamilton having a good season, and he suffered a season-ending quad injury last week. Henry had a good start to the season, but has had some quiet games lately with the Ravens suffering through bad quarterback play. He has 156 yards on 45 carries (3.5-yard average) over the last three weeks, and Baltimore hasn't scored more than 13 points in any of those games. But they need Henry to have a dominant performance Thursday, and that won't be easy against a good Jaguars defense.

The Jaguars travel to Baltimore to take on the M.A.S.H. unit that is the Ravens' remaining roster on Thursday night. Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley? Both out on IR. Third-stringer Joe Fagnano will start, but is dealing with an ankle injury. Baltimore is reportedly trading for Jacoby Brissett, but he won't be playing this week. To put a little context into the state of both teams, this line was Ravens -4.5 this offseason, it re-opened Ravens +8 on Sunday and got as high as +10. The Jaguars are only 4-3 against the spread this season, but they're coming off a 42-7 rout of an undermanned Colts team. Baltimore has talent on both sides of the ball, but this is a big ask for Fagnano — on a short week and with an injury no less. There are some Jacksonville -9.5s out there in the market. I'd jump on one of those or put Jacksonville in a 7-point tease with the 49ers -9.5 to get both under a field goal. — Ben Fawkes

#2842. Bronny James assigned to G League's Coachella Valley Lakers after playing limited minutes early in third NBA season

Bronny James made the Los Angeles Lakers' 2026-27 opening night roster, securing a spot while his draft classmate, first-round pick Dalton Knecht, was waived.

But after James averaged 2.4 minutes in five appearances amid the Lakers' 7-2 start, the team announced Thursday that it assigned the 2024 second-round pick to the G League's Coachella Valley Lakers.

Fellow guard Cameron Carr was also sent to the franchise's G League affiliate ahead of its Friday night season opener against the Utah Jazz's Salt Lake City Stars.

Bronny James — in his first season in the Lakers' organization without his father, LeBron James — previously spent time in the G League, where his stat lines have often dwarfed those he has authored in the NBA.

Across a combined 25 regular-season games the past two seasons with the then-South Bay Lakers — who have since relocated and rebranded — Bronny James posted 18.4 points, 4.4 assists, 4 rebounds and 1.5 steals per game while shooting 49.6% from the field, including 41.3% from beyond the arc.

James, now 22, was the No. 55 pick when he was drafted out of USC more than two years ago. He appeared in 27 regular-season games with the Lakers as a rookie, making father-son history but struggling mightily at times, particularly early on.

He made noticeable progress in Year 2, taking the court for 42 regular-season games and raising his field-goal percentage from 31.3% (2024-25) to 40.9% (2025-26) and his 3-point percentage from 28.1% (2024-25) to 38.6% (2025-26).

Head coach JJ Redick saw enough from James to include him in his playoff rotation last season, albeit a shorthanded one.

That paved the way for additional excitement and milestones.

Coming into Year 3, the question was: How would James fare without his superstar father on the Lakers (including how would he be treated by staff and teammates in the absence of the future Hall of Famer)?

But James hasn't been given much of an opportunity in the regular season to make any kind of jump. In his sparing minutes, he scored 0.8 points per game. He attempted only three field goals and made two of them.

James could wind up back with the Lakers soon, but his lack of on-court involvement so far isn't promising, in terms of his chances of carving out the long-term 3-and-D role he teased on occasion last season.

As for Knecht, he ended up signing a two-way deal with the New York Knicks, for whom he logged 10 points on 4-of-6 shooting, plus seven rebounds, on Wednesday in a win over the Chicago Bulls.

#2841. Why Grizzlies’ Isaiah Stewart has ‘no regrets’ on wild LeBron James altercation

Why Grizzlies' Isaiah Stewart has 'no regrets' on wild LeBron James altercation appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Isaiah Stewart has built a reputation as one of the NBA's most fiery players, and the Memphis Grizzlies big man is not about to apologize for it. Looking back on his infamous altercation with LeBron James and other run-ins, Stewart made it clear he does not regret a thing.

In an interview with Andscape, Stewart was asked whether he had any regrets about those incidents or if it was simply the emotion of the moment.

"I don't have no regrets. You know, I feel like there's lessons out of everything. You know, even going back to that first incident, you know, Charlotte, like I don't have no regrets and I don't care what people may perceive me as," Stewart said. "I come to play basketball and I play the game a certain way. And it has been times where, you know, I thought back and say, hey, I shouldn't have done that."

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Stewart also called out opponents he believes try to bait him into losing his cool.

"And I feel like teams having their game plan of, hey, let's do something to trip him off, to get him out the game. For me, it's hard to respect you as a man if that's your game plan," he said.

The LeBron incident remains the most memorable of Stewart's career. Back in November 2021, James struck Stewart in the face with an elbow while the two were battling for position during a free throw, leaving the then-Detroit Pistons big man bloodied. An enraged Stewart repeatedly charged after James and had to be held back. James was ejected and later suspended one game, while Stewart received a two-game suspension.

His most recent suspension came in February 2026, when Stewart left the bench to join a fight between the Pistons and the Charlotte Hornets. The NBA handed him a seven-game ban, citing his "repeated history of unsportsmanlike acts." It was his fourth suspension for fighting.

A few months later, Detroit traded Stewart to the Grizzlies for three future second-round picks during the 2026 NBA Draft, ending his six-season run with the Pistons.

Now 25 years old, Stewart is part of a Grizzlies team that is off to a 6-2 start. Based on his comments, he has no plans of changing the way he plays anytime soon.

#2836. George Kittle is skeptical that Brandon Aiyuk will "get in" the team facility

As 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk attempts to lay the foundation for a return to the NFL, at least one other 49ers player has expressed skepticism about Aiyuk's plan.

His new agent, Drew Rosenhaus, has said that Aiyuk will report to the 49ers' facility on Friday. Tight end George Kittle shared his views regarding that strategy on Thursday when asked whether he's looking forward to seeing Aiyuk.

"Do you think he gets in the facility?" Kittle said, via Grant Cohn of SI.com.

Kittle then was asked if he thinks Aiyuk gets into the facility.

"Plead the fifth," Kittle said. "Yeah. You can ask [G.M.] John [Lynch]. Or [coach] Kyle [Shanahan]. Don't ask me. I'm a conspiracy guy."

"Is there a conspiracy?" Kittle was asked.

Kittle's skepticism is warranted. The entire situation is strange, and awkward. Aiyuk clearly hopes to trigger his outright release by reporting for work, after months of making it clear he had no intention to do so.

The 49ers are tiptoeing around the situation, for now. Before the team has to do anything, Aiyuk needs to check the various boxes that put the 49ers in a posture that requires a decision.

Of all potential outcomes, the least likely involves Aiyuk being welcomed back onto the roster. Especially since Aiyuk likely has no desire to be on the roster.

He wants his freedom, and he finally — after months of stubbornness — has initiated the process aimed at making that happen. While it's possible that the 49ers will search for a hardball outcome that involves keeping him from becoming a free agent while avoiding putting him back on the team, the chances of Aiyuk wandering around the locker room and eventually wearing a 49ers' helmet again seem to be incredibly slim.

Patriots owner Robert Kraft recently underwent back surgery, which will keep him sidelined on “injured reserve” and force him to miss Rob Gronkowski’s induction into the Patriots Hall of Fame on Saturday.

#2833. The Golics Shows How ESPN Can Make Radio Content Work Well on Television

Between layoffs and general personnel changes, there seems to be a constant shift in the personalities we see across ESPN's multiple platforms. One unexpected move over the past year was forming The Golics, and the return of Mike Golic, who made a name for himself as one half of the Mike & Mike ESPN Radio show, which ran from 2000 to 2017.

On that show, Golic teamed with ESPN star Mike Greenberg in what became a historically popular national sports program. In his latest incarnation at ESPN, Golic teams with his son, Mike Golic Jr., on The Golics. An ESPN Radio show that also airs periodically on the network's television platforms.

I caught an episode this past week with two main goals.

First, I wanted to see if the senior Golic still had his sports talk fastball. Second, I wanted to see if this was a worthy vehicle for Golic Jr., who had previously bounced around in several roles at ESPN.

Both questions were answered with a resounding yes.

The show essentially started with a cold open featuring an extremely enthusiastic Golic Jr. rehashing the Kansas City Chiefs-Denver Broncos' tightly contested matchup from the previous weekend. The presentation is essentially a two-shot, with Golic Jr. on the left and his dad on the right.

There is a certain charm to this father-son combination. It brings a warm, family feel to the program.

On The Golics, highlights are interspersed with discussion. While graphics provide pertinent statistics on the topics being discussed. It was certainly nice to see and hear the elder Golic again. His return to the ESPN airwaves is most welcome after stops at both FanDuel and DraftKings.

Golic is an old-school, straight-up, hard-nosed analyst. The one who is going to tell you what he thinks, whether you like it or not. The father and son not only fit together as family members but also complement each other stylistically. Golic Jr. comes right at you with a powerful voice and equally strong opinions.

It is not all hardcore football talk with The Golics, either. Coming back from a break, they had some fun with a clip of LeBron James going into the crowd and taking a sip of a fan's wine before returning to the court. That led to a conversation about whether wine should even be served at a sporting event. This subject was right in the elder Golic's wheelhouse. On Mike & Mike, he was definitely the comedic wild card alongside Greenberg, the ultimate straight man.

The Golics also had a rather seriocomic discussion about the size and cost of muffins. Topics ventured into music and other areas, giving the show some variety.

However, what the Golics don't do is belabor the humor. They move through it quickly before bringing the conversation back to sports. This past week, the duo segued into their Golic Gamers awards, recognizing players who turned in outstanding performances over the weekend.

For me, the best part of the show is the forum it provides for Golic Jr. It is difficult to follow in the footsteps of an established sports media star who also happens to be your father. Just ask Jac Collinsworth, Joe Buck, Noah Eagle, the Caray family and many others.

Golic Jr. runs this show expertly, moving from topic to topic with verve and passion.

Golic Sr. offered some strong analysis of Gardner Minshew. He was coming off a tremendous game for the Cardinals last weekend, going 17-of-23 for 188 passing yards and three touchdowns in a comeback win over the Cowboys. Golic highlighted Minshew's performance, and his comments proved somewhat prophetic. The Cardinals subsequently handed Minshew the reins and agreed to trade former starter Jacoby Brissett to Baltimore.

While analyzing the Bears' 34-17 victory over the Seahawks on Monday Night Football, Golic Jr. said Chicago might be the best team in the NFC and was definitely the best team in the NFC North "with a bullet."

I like what the father and son bring to the table on this program. The connection they share as family members and former football players creates a more personal and engaging discourse when discussing sports.

ESPN, along with other networks, periodically airs many of its radio shows and podcasts on television. On these programs, visual variety is at a premium. Too often, the presentation consists of a single shot ad nauseam. On The Golics, graphics, highlights and audio clips help address that issue, providing much-needed visual breaks from the static two-shot.

When discussing the Baltimore Ravens' quarterback situation, Golic Jr. referenced facts that many viewers may not have known. Specifically, he pointed out that Lamar Jackson has considerable leverage in his contract situation because his deal precludes the Ravens from using either the franchise or transition tag on him.

Jackson also has a full no-trade clause and negotiates his own deals. It was a good tidbit from Golic Jr. that added concrete information to the speculation surrounding Jackson's future in Baltimore.

As the show closed, The Golics continued offering their views on several NFL teams. I particularly enjoyed their exchange about the Jacksonville Jaguars and why they deserve more attention. The Golics is an excellent combination of opinion and debate, effectively blending facts and fun with a unique family flair.

Barrett Media produces daily content on the music, news, and sports media industries. Sign up for our newsletters to stay updated and get the latest information right in your inbox.

#2831. As Chargers' offense struggles, Mike McDaniel will move from the sideline to the booth

Chargers offensive coordinator Mike McDaniel will get a different perspective on his struggling offense on Sunday against the Texans.

McDaniel said today that he will move upstairs to the booth to call plays after previously working from the sideline.

“One of the things that I want to try is call plays from the box to see adjustments for myself and communicate with the position coaches appropriately what things we can focus on between series,” McDaniel said. “I won’t be in the same spot.”

Although the Chargers’ offense has been among the worst in the NFL this season and head coach Jim Harbaugh has faced questions about whether he should fire his offensive coordinator, McDaniel insisted that he’s not concerned about the criticism.

“My problem solving doesn’t come from insecurity,” McDaniel said. “I’m not trying to prove or justify anything.”

The Chargers rank dead last in the NFL in scoring, having scored only 99 points this season, and they have an NFL-high 17 turnovers. McDaniel said he thinks it’s worth trying something different while delegating some of what he had been doing on the sideline to the assistant coaches.

“Seems like a good action to take based on how things have gone this year, and realistically something that I’ve only brought up because I’ve observed, I’m very confident in the rest of the offensive coaching staff and how they’re able to set the tone and communicate information on the sidelines. So I feel a little more comfortable to try that,” McDaniel said.

If that doesn’t work, Harbaugh’s next step might be to move McDaniel from the booth to the unemployment line.

#2830. Yankees Trade Pitch Lands Jazz Chisholm Replacement in 4-For-1 White Sox Deal

Chase Meidroth #10 of the Chicago White Sox would go to the Yankees in a new trade pitch. Getty

Anthony Volpe had to Chicago under the framework of a trade pitch published Thursday. Getty

Jazz Chisholm Jr. is headed for free agency without a qualifying offer from the New York Yankees, and a trade pitch published Thursday by Baseball Trade Values and aimed at the Yankees would replace him with a former Boston Red Sox prospect.

The Yankees' $23.15 million qualifying offer to David Bednar came Monday, but none went to Chisholm, a strong indication that the Yankees are fully prepared to let Chisholm walk in free agency, opening a second base vacancy. But would White Sox second baseman Chase Meidroth be an upgrade?

The site, which grades proposed deals with numerical values assigned to each player, would send Anthony Volpe, Elmer Rodriguez, Will Warren and Ben Hess to the Chicago White Sox, valuing that package at 53.4 in surplus against 42.2 for Meidroth.

GettyChase Meidroth #10 of the Chicago White Sox would go to the Yankees in a new trade pitch.

Chisholm, 28, a two-time All-Star born in Nassau, Bahamas, arrived from Miami in July 2024 for catcher Agustín Ramírez and two infielders. He hit .231/.312/.399 with 19 homers and a career-high 41 steals this season, but struck out in 29.5% of his plate appearances. His glove graded at plus-15 Outs Above Average and minus-6 Defensive Runs Saved.

A year earlier, Chisholm slashed .242/.332/.481 with 31 homers. He then went 2-for-11 against Tampa Bay in the division series, made a throwing error in Game 2 and sat two games.

"This is where I want to be," Chisholm said after the sweep, as quoted by the New York Post. Former MLB general manager Jim Bowden was less sure, saying on "Foul Territory" he would "probably pass on both" Chisholm and Ketel Marte if he were still running a baseball organization.

Chisholm earned $10.2 million this season. He reportedly sought $35 million annually over eight to 10 years, while an executive projected three or four years near $20 million, according to MLB.com. Meidroth earned $789,100 last season, and is not eligible for arbitration until 2028.

GettyAnthony Volpe had to Chicago under the framework of a trade pitch published Thursday.

Meidroth, 25, a Torrance, California, native and University of San Diego product, was a Red Sox fourth-round pick in 2022 who arrived at the White Sox in the Garrett Crochet trade, and hit .276/.350/.415 with 16 homers in 2026. His .765 OPS topped Chisholm's .711, and his 4.5 fWAR led American League second basemen, a full win above Chisholm's 3.5.

Chisholm owns the edge in power and speed.

The price is four players. Rodriguez, 23, from Trujillo Alto, Puerto Rico, is the No. 2 prospect in the system and went 0-3 with a 5.40 ERA in six starts, striking out 21 over 26⅔ innings. The Red Sox drafted him in the fourth round in 2021, and he came from Boston for Carlos Narvaez.

Volpe, 25, a Manhattan native and 2019 first-rounder, won the 2023 AL Gold Glove at shortstop. Volpe hit .212/.272/.391 in 2025 and returned from shoulder surgery to bat .245 with two homers in 2026, after signing a one-year, $3.48 million contract.

Warren, 27, a Brandon, Mississippi, native and eighth-round pick out of Southeastern Louisiana, went 11-6 with a 3.92 ERA and 158 strikeouts in 156 innings.

Hess, 24, from Charleston, Illinois, was the 26th overall pick in 2024 out of Alabama and posted a 4.45 ERA over 56⅔ innings before the 60-day injured list ended his year, after a 3.22 ERA across 103⅓ innings in 2025.

Competitively, Chicago gets a starter, a former Gold Glove shortstop and two pitching prospects, while New York fills second base but loses Warren's 29 starts. By the site's model, Chicago wins by 11.2 points, and Warren alone makes up more than half of Meidroth's 42.2. The two clubs rarely deal. A seven-player trade in July 2017 brought Todd Frazier, David Robertson and Tommy Kahnle to New York. The latest sent reliever Keynan Middleton to New York for Juan Carela on Aug. 1, 2023.

#2829. John Lynch on Brandon Aiyuk: We'll look for a path that works for both sides

Brandon Aiyuk filed for reinstatement from the 49ers’ left squad list this week and he’s expected to be at the team’s facility on Friday to take the next step in resuming his football career.

Once he is reinstated, the 49ers will have a choice between keeping him on the active roster, trading him or releasing him. The latter option is seen as the likeliest given the lack of trade interest earlier this year and both head coach Kyle Shanahan and General Manager John Lynch saying that Aiyuk is done playing for the Niners.

Shanahan said on Wednesday that his thinking hasn’t changed and Lynch said on KNBR Thursday that the team is working with the league to come up with the best solution.

“Look, I think the way I’ll characterize it, number one, we’ve got a 6-1 football team that we think has really got a shot at this thing this year,” Lynch said. “So our focus is going to remain on this team. Kyle spoke of that yesterday. Having said that, I have a job to do, and it entails a lot of different things. This is kind of a unique situation, not one you have — so there’s really no playbook, but I always go back to what’s my charge, what’s my job. My job is to look out for the best interests of the 49ers, and as such, in this particular situation, we’re working with the NFL management council to navigate through kind of a different situation. It’s fluid. I’m going to kinda leave it at that because I’ve been recommended to do so, and I think it’s the right thing to do. We’ll look for a pathway for both sides here that works. Next week we can talk a little more in detail about the process because kind of a fluid situation.”

Lynch said generally that anything is possible when asked if there’s any way Aiyuk could return to the team and said the wideout’s new agent Drew Rosenhaus “is a good guy to deal with” before reiterating that he’d have more to say in the future about the entire situation.

The Ravens’ inactives are quarterback Kedon Slovis, wide receiver Elijah Sarratt, offensive lineman Emery Jones Jr., defensive lineman Aeneas Peebles, cornerback Nate Wiggins (stinger/illness) and safety Jaylinn Hawkins (knee). Slovis is the emergency third quarterback, meaning Austin Reed, who the Ravens signed on Monday, will be the No. 2 quarterback behind Joe Fagnano.

#2827. Elder's Kaden Estep is rewriting Panther records in senior season

Nearly 40 years ago, Steve Keller dominated the Elder Panther quarterback records. The starting quarterback in 1987 and 1988, Keller amassed 4,816 yards and 45 touchdowns in his career.

Twenty years after Keller, Mark Miller led Elder to the Division I state championship game in 2008 and the semifinals in 2009. Along the way, he broke both of Keller's career records with 5,872 yards and 56 touchdowns.

Miller's touchdown record still stands, but his yardage record didn't get that 20-year cushion. First, Peyton Ramsey broke it with 6,708 yards (and took second with 49 touchdowns) from 2013 to 2015, but the Elder record books were rewritten again in 2026 and the pencil is still sharpened to take down another.

Elder senior quarterback and Cincinnati Bearcats commit Kaden Estep broke Ramsey's record with a 13-yard pass to Jake Bier in a 62-6 win over Mount Zion Prep on Oct. 23. Estep finished the game with 6,861 career passing yards. His five touchdown passes in that game put him at 53 for his career, just three behind Miller.

"Earned," Ramsey, now an assistant coach at Elder, posted on X after Estep's record-breaking night. "The greatest QB to ever put on the uniform. Period. An absolute winner. All of it a byproduct of the type of teammate he is. Keep going!"

Since Estep took over as a sophomore in 2024, Elder has gone 26-9, including an undefeated 2025 regular season and Greater Catholic-League South championship. It was Elder's first GCL-South crown since Ramsey led Elder to a split with St. Xavier in 2015 and first solo title since Miller led the Panthers in '08.

Miller, now a teacher at Taylor, was previously the head coach for the Yellowjackets after coaching junior varsity at Elder. Seeing Ramsey initially break his yardage record was a full-circle moment.

"I remember Peyton and I remember one of (assistant coach) Craig James' sons, Jakob, being ball boys and running around," Miller said. "Your typical coach's kid that Peyton is has been living, breathing and eating Elder football since the time he could walk, I'm sure.

"It's been fun to watch to kind of watch him progress from the player that I got to watch after I graduated to the coach and the teacher that he is now."

Miller got an early look at what Coach Peyton Ramsey would look like when Ramsey was just starting his college career at Indiana.

"I texted him," Miller said. "I was like, 'Hey, I need your help on drawing some of these plays up because I've been removed far enough now where all this language is new.' When I asked (Elder head coach) Doug (Ramsey), I said, 'Doug, do you have a playbook for me?' And I'll never forget it; he literally pointed to his head and that was it. I was like, 'Well, I'm going to need some help from the guy who was just here.'

"Even then and obviously as a player, he was special and could break things down and do things a lot better than other players in the past have. Just seeing how cerebral he was at the time as a 19-, 20-year-old kid, you know he could have coached that offense while he was playing and certainly while he was in college."

Ramsey is in his sixth season as an assistant for his dad. He and Estep are the only two quarterbacks to be three-year starters in Doug's 30 seasons as Elder head coach.

The younger Ramsey ‒ who still clears all other Elder QBs with 9,400 total yards of offense, according to Elder statistician Kelby Siler ‒ has worked closely with Estep to turn him from a good sophomore to an all-time great high school quarterback.

Estep had 1,908 yards and 12 touchdowns to eight interceptions as a sophomore in 2024 when Elder went 6-6. He also ran for 189 yards on 91 carries with two touchdowns and six fumbles.

The next season, Estep led Elder to a 12-1 record and a trip to the regional finals. He threw for 2,430 yards and 15 touchdowns and ran for 924 more yards and 10 touchdowns on 131 carries.

Estep's senior season, with an upcoming playoff game against Springboro, has seen him flip a Miami RedHawks commitment to a Cincinnati Bearcats commitment. The Bearcat offer came two days after a loss to St. Xavier, where he completed 20-of-26 passes for 236 yards and three touchdowns, with 72 rushing yards and another score.

In 10 games in 2026, Estep has completed 164 of 223 passes for 2,523 yards and 26 touchdowns, with just three picks. He has run for 780 yards and eight touchdowns.

Estep needs three touchdowns to tie Miller for the career mark, a number Estep has done eight times in his career, including five times this season.

This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Elder's Kaden Estep is rewriting Panther records in senior season

It was the only third down the Jaguars faced on the drive.

Trevor Lawrence was 3-for-4 for 72 yards, including a 52-yarder to rookie tight end Nate Boerkircher. Boerkircher got lost by the defense as Lawrence rolled to his right to avoid pressure.

Lawrence also threw a 16-yard pass to Jakobi Meyers.

#2824. Joe Milton on his first start: The NFL is about being ready when your number is called

Joe Milton has never started an NFL game. The closest he came was in Week 18 of the 2024 season when Patriots starter Drake Maye played only three snaps before giving way to Milton, who played the final 61 snaps.

Milton looked like a starting NFL quarterback in that game, completing 22 of 29 passes for 241 yards and a touchdown.

Less than two years later, Milton is on his third team and has played only 51 snaps in four appearances with Dallas since.

He will make his first career start on Sunday when the Eagles play the Giants.

“I was just pretty much going through the plays, going through the week like I was a starter, or like I was playing, and just being ready,” Milton said Thursday, via Olivia Reiner of the Philadelphia Inquirer. “And that’s what the NFL is about: Just being ready when your name is called, and just preparing for those moments.”

The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys’ practice squad on Oct. 16 after they placed Jalen Hurts on injured reserve due to blood clots. Tanner McKee started the past three games, but went 0-3 as the Eagles ran their losing streak to six games.

Milton has had a crash course in learning the playbook and the terminology, which he said reminds him of the West Coast scheme he had in New England his rookie season.

Milton was asked how much of the Eagles’ playbook he knows.

“Shoot, whatever’s in this week,” he said with a laugh.

#2823. Baltimore Ravens vs. Jacksonville Jaguars, ‘TNF’ Week 9 open thread

Tonight's game between the Baltimore Ravens and Jacksonville Jaguars once held a lot of promise as a truly great matchup in primetime. Now? Well… let's just say the shine has worn off Baltimore's season with quarterbacks Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley both going on Injured Reserve.

Yes, the Ravens lost two quarterbacks to significant injuries in their loss to the Buffalo Bills. Adding insult here, emergency quarterback Joe Fagnano also picked up an injury in relief. Despite that, Fagnano gets his first official NFL start tonight as Week 9 gets underway with Thursday Night Football.

It's a massive challenge for Fagnano, who must try to lead a nose-diving Ravens teams to a win against a Jacksonville Jaguars team that seems as well-rounded as any in the league. With time to prep as the team's QB1, Fagnano should look a little more game ready. There's scant professional film on him, but what there is shows a quarterback who couldn't solve the Bills' defense in Week 8.

Baltimore's coaching staff will keep their fingers crossed that things go well enough, otherwise the team will be forced to turn to quarterback Kevon Slovis. It feels like we're set for a blowout this evening, but strange things do happen on Thursday nights.

Do the Ravens roll out the smart play and run everything on offense through running back Derrick Henry? It's not a terrible idea, but they don't seem to have quite the offensive line and blocking Henry enjoyed in prior seasons. Would it be terrible if they better-incorporated Henry in the passing attack? You'd find no argument from me on the matter.

This is a huge challenge for the Ravens against a 5-2 Jaguars team we've barely discussed. Jacksonville has an enormous lead in the AFC South, with the 2-5 Houston Texans in second among a mostly moribund division. One of few teams to defeat the Bills at home during the playoffs, there's little love lost between Bills Mafia and those who cry "Duuuuval."

That's right, Rumblers: Tonight you're Ravens fans. Buffalo stands to benefit from a Jaguars loss, even though the Bills currently have the top spot in the AFC race to the playoffs. With that, here's your open thread for Week 9 of Thursday Night Football. As always, be kind to one another — and in all things… Go Bills!

Kim Mulkey and the No. 9 LSU Tigers showed absolutely no mercy against their opponents in Texas Southern, making college basketball history for the ages with a remarkable blowout win on Thursday night.

Leverkusen have won seven of their 15 meetings against French clubs, but they lost 7-2 to PSG at BayArena in last season's Champions League campaign. This is the first time they face a team from Europe's top-five leagues in the league phase of the Europa League this term.

#2818. Thunder’s Latest Signing Reveals Unexpected Alex Caruso Connection

PHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA - MARCH 10: Dalen Terry #14 of the Philadelphia 76ers dribbles the ball during a game against the Memphis Grizzlies at Xfinity Mobile Arena on March 10, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Emilee Chinn/Getty Images) Getty

The Oklahoma City Thunder may have uncovered more than additional roster depth with their latest signing. General manager Sam Presti brought Dalen Terry to Oklahoma City, reuniting the former Chicago Bulls first-round pick with someone who helped shape his NBA career.

That player is Alex Caruso, one of the Thunder's most valuable defensive veterans. Terry recently revealed that Caruso was more than a teammate during their time together in Chicago. He was a mentor whose playing style became a blueprint for Terry's development.

"I always thought my niche in the league could be like Alex Caruso," Terry said in comments shared by Full Court Press. "And even though we're a little different, I just think that I could see him and I could see myself."

Terry explained that Caruso regularly helped him understand defensive responsibilities and prepare for the demands of an NBA season. That relationship began early in Terry's career, when both players were members of the Bulls.

"So it was always easy to ask him in the off-seasons what he did to prepare for the seasons and even in the game, different coverages and stuff," Terry said.

The comparison becomes particularly interesting when examining their career production per 36 minutes, a statistic that estimates what players might produce with equal playing time.

GettyPHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA – MARCH 10: Dalen Terry #14 of the Philadelphia 76ers dribbles the ball during a game against the Memphis Grizzlies at Xfinity Mobile Arena on March 10, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Emilee Chinn/Getty Images)

Caruso has averaged approximately 11.2 points, 4.8 rebounds, 4.5 assists and 2.2 steals per 36 minutes. Terry's corresponding figures are 11.3 points, 5.3 rebounds, 3.9 assists and 1.6 steals.

Those similarities deserve attention, although they do not establish that Terry offers the same impact. Caruso has built his reputation through elite defensive anticipation, positioning and performance in meaningful games. Terry still must demonstrate that his production translates into a consistent role.

Nevertheless, Terry's rebounding, passing and defensive activity suggest there is something worth developing.

Oklahoma City officially announced Terry's multiyear signing Wednesday, adding a 24-year-old wing with 218 games of NBA experience. Injuries to several important rotation players have increased the Thunder's need for additional depth.

What makes Terry particularly intriguing is his familiarity with the defensive philosophy Caruso represents. Oklahoma City prioritizes versatility, communication and players willing to contribute without dominating possessions.

Caruso's mentorship could help Terry adapt to those expectations.

"And A.C.'s always been a coach on the court," Terry said. "Even my rookie year and my second year when he was there, he always helped me. So I always appreciate him for that."

Terry is not another Caruso simply because their statistics resemble one another. However, learning directly from an established defensive specialist gives him a meaningful foundation.

For Presti and the Thunder, that connection could make this signing considerably more valuable than an ordinary depth addition.

#2816. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

#2814. Derrick Henry moves to seventh on the NFL's all-time rushing list

The Ravens haven’t had much to celebrate Thursday night, but Derrick Henry gave the home fans something to cheer.

Henry had 51 first-half rushing yards, which was enough to move him into seventh on the all-time rushing list. He passed Jerome Bettis (13,662) and LaDainian Tomlinson (13,684) in moving from ninth to seventh.

Curtis Martin is next on the list, with his 14,101 career rushing yards ranking sixth all time.

Henry entered the night with 632 yards for the season and 13,650 in his career.

Over the past four seasons, Henry has averaged 1,578 yards. He has averaged 85 yards per game in his career.

Henry will need to play another three to four years to break Emmitt Smith’s record of 18,355 yards.

Smith was 35 years old in his final season of 2004. Henry turns 33 in January.

Smith said earlier this year that he believes Henry has a chance to break his record.

#2813. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2812. The 1 trade Bengals must make in final week before 2026 NFL trade deadline

The 1 trade Bengals must make in final week before 2026 NFL trade deadline appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Do the Cincinnati Bengals have one more trade in them this season? They've already made one of the biggest trades this season by acquiring ex-New York Giants defensive tackle Dexter Lawrence during the 2026 NFL Draft. Despite that blockbuster move, though, Cincinnati isn't quite satisfied with where their team currently stands amongst the contenders in the AFC.

Because of that, the Bengals are involved in some trade rumors as the trade deadline approaches. There are plenty of trades that Cincinnati can make to bolster their roster. Let's look at the move that this team should make.

The Bengals traded for Lawrence, yes, but it was clear that the defense still had a ton of weaknesses. In particular, their linebacker group was a major question mark coming into the season. Barrett Carter and Demetrius Knight Jr. have both played better this season, but it's still painfully clear that both linebackers are outmatched against their opponents. Opposing offenses have feasted in the short and intermediate areas of the field due to the duo's struggles in coverage.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

That's the reason why Bengals fans were clamoring for the team to sign or trade for a linebacker during the offseason. Popular choices at the time include Dolphins LB Jordyn Brooks and now-Washington Commanders LB Leo Chenal. However, another more intriguing option has emerged this season as a potential trade target: Philadelphia Eagles linebacker Zack Baun.

One of the best linebackers in the league, Baun's availability is tied heavily to the Eagles' fate this season. With Jalen Hurts out for the year due to his deep vein thrombosis, Philadelphia has gone from contenders to potential sellers at the trade deadline. Tanner McKee has done nothing to inspire confidence in a playoff run, and they're now taking a look at prospect Joe Milton to lead them the rest of the day. Because of that and the long-term implications of Hurts' DVT diagnosis, many are wondering if Philadelphia might decide to enter a rebuild.

Baun is one of the players who will likely fetch good value at the trade deadline. The Eagles linebacker started his career as an edge rusher, but he has made the transition to a coverage linebacker with the Eagles. Baun is one of the best coverage linebackers in the league, able to cover both tight ends and slot receivers in space. He was part of the 2024 Eagles team that won the Super Bowl, so he also brings championship pedigree to a Bengals team that has high aspirations this season.

Cincinnati's linebackers are playing better than last year, but last year is an admittedly low standard that they have barely cleared this season. Knight Jr. has been great against the run this season, but he's one of the worst linebackers in coverage this year. Carter has been great for the most part too, but he's still prone to lapses in coverage and takes bad angles at times. Having Baun, a bona fide veteran, as one of their linebackers would be greatly helpful to this team moving forward.

#2807. Minkah Fitzpatrick clears air on ‘Jets being Jets’ controversy during losing streak

There hasn't been much good news around the Jets this season, and their misery was amplified after Sunday's loss to the Raiders when veteran safety Minkah Fitzpatrick appeared to have said the team's defensive mistakes were "Jets being Jets."

But Fitzpatrick reiterated Thursday he instead said it was the "Jets beating Jets," referring to self-inflicted wounds due to penalties and miscues.

For a team that entered the season with playoff aspirations and enters Sunday's game in Kansas City just 2-6, having lost six of seven and three in a row, it was an unwanted distraction that Fitzpatrick said didn't impact the mood of the locker room.

"It hasn't really caused any stir within this locker room,'' Fitzpatrick said. "That's the most important thing. Everyone in this locker room knew what I was talking about. If you look at the context of what I said, if you look at what I've been saying all season, we've been addressing 'Jets not beating Jets' or 'Jets beating Jets.' Everyone in this locker room knows exactly what I said, what I was trying to communicate, and in my opinion, that's all that matters."

That, and maybe getting a win for the first time since they beat the Browns on Oct. 11.

Sunday, they'll visit a Chiefs team that has dropped its past two after opening the year with five consecutive wins.

"I know what I said,'' Fitzpatrick said. "It doesn't matter. We're playing Kansas City this week. … This locker room needs to focus on winning right now — and not something that I might have not said or something I might have done. Our focus and objective now is to win and not focus on should've, would've, could've."

Jets safety Minkah Fitzpatrick speaks to the media after practice in Florham Park, N.J., Thursday, Nov. 5, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY Post

Fitzpatrick said the controversy impacted him more because it had the potential to distract the team from what they have to do on the field to try to salvage something of what's been another ugly season.

"It more so bothered me in the moment, when we're a team that's fighting to win and we're talking about stuff that doesn't really matter, stuff that doesn't factor into victory,'' Fitzpatrick said.

Garrett Wilson, who's been in concussion protocol, did not practice again Thursday. If he can't go Sunday in Kansas City, the offense will look considerably different — even if he has occasionally lacked targets.

"In one respect, you can't replace Garrett Wilson,'' offensive coordinator Frank Reich said. "He's a unique player. But on the other hand, we're not afraid. We saw the game [Adonai Mitchell] had. We saw the game [Omar Cooper Jr.] had. We have a lot of confidence in our guys. They're different players, so maybe you do slightly different things. If Garrett's up, we'll do Garrett things. If he's not, we'll do [Mitchell] things or [Cooper] things. You have to play to your players' strengths."

David Bailey was a full participant Thursday after being limited Wednesday with a groin injury.

After the defense was torched by Las Vegas tight end Brock Bowers on Sunday, defensive coordinator Brian Duker was asked Thursday what he expected from Kansas City and Travis Kelce.

"I wouldn't anticipate reading their minds on that,'' Duker said, before adding of Kelce, "He's probably used a little differently [than Bowers]. Obviously, both are very good, but they're probably used a little differently."

#2805. Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1

Observations after Sixers beat Bulls to reach East-leading 7-1 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers kept up their hot start to the season Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, reaching 7-1 with a 126-107 win against the Bulls.

All five of the Sixers' starters scored in double figures. Tyrese Maxey posted 26 points on 10-for-15 shooting and 11 assists. Jaylen Brown tallied a season-best 25 points and 11 rebounds.

VJ Edgecombe's 21 points were a new season high. LeBron James recorded 15 points, nine assists and seven boards.

Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson had 24 points and seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid (15 points, eight rebounds) had been listed as questionable with an official designation of "right foot plantar fascia injury management." He sat out Saturday on the second night of a back-to-back for the Sixers' win over the Hornets.

"Plantar is a little bit of a come and go type of thing," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters pregame. "They're just trying to manage it. It's very painful, if you've ever had it. It can kind of come and go out of nowhere. So we're just trying to manage the pain with that, be as smart as we can."

Nurse told reporters that Dominick Barlow is set to see a specialist Tuesday about the back injury that's sidelined him to start the season.

"He's been on court a little bit … not a ton of moving around," Nurse said. "I think they want to get to tomorrow's appointment. Hopefully, it'll be positive and we can get into his workouts a little bit."

The Sixers will begin a three-game road trip Wednesday when they visit the Pistons. Here are observations on their victory Monday:

After Embiid blitzed a Bulls pick-and-roll, Maxey picked off Matas Buzelis' hurried pass to get the Sixers rolling in transition and James jammed home the team's first field goal.

Edgecombe got a slam soon after James grabbed a defensive rebound and the Sixers again pushed the pace. The Sixers scored six quick fast-break points, leading the Bulls to burn an early timeout, and James' long-distance feeds were a major asset.

"If you're open, he's going to pass it," Edgecombe said of James. "The main thing is just being ready. Tonight I had to run and go get 'em. If you're open and he sees you, he's going to pass the ball."

Edgecombe had a dunk-heavy first quarter. He threw down his second when Dean Wade nabbed a steal and dropped off a typically selfless dish to the Sixers' explosive second-year guard, whose slam put the team up 22-13.

The Sixers have an abundance of dangerous options in the open floor, Edgecombe included. As he's already shown with his continued clutch shotmaking, the 21-year-old won't become anything resembling a non-factor offensively this year as a byproduct of the Sixers' enhanced star power.

Over the team's first seven games, Edgecombe had averaged 11.3 points. He scored 11 in the first period on 5-for-5 shooting.

Adem Bona missed a dunk early in the second quarter, which stung a bit more when Jalen Smith drilled a three-pointer seconds later.

Bona remained the Sixers' main backup center. He had a poor first-half stint overall amid a largely negative start to his third season. The 23-year-old big man played well in the Sixers' victory over the Bucks, posting six points, 13 rebounds and three blocks. Outside of that, his season has been sparse on bright moments so far. It's reasonable to wonder when Nurse might turn to alternatives like Jabari Walker, who played a good game Saturday in Charlotte, or try James again at the five.

The Bulls outscored the Sixers by eight points in Bona's first run, cutting their derficit to 40-38. Chicago went in front with a three by former Sixer Buddy Hield.

The Sixers regained the advantage late in the second quarter. Maxey and James each were excellent as passers in the first half, combining for 14 assists and three turnovers. James fired the ball to Wade for a corner three that gave the Sixers a 60-52 lead.

Following a third-quarter Embiid fadeaway jumper and two James lay-ins, the Sixers were up 17 points.

The Bulls cut the Sixers' lead down to 11 points by the start of the fourth quarter. With Bona still struggling and on four fouls, Wade began the fourth at center. Bona then briefly spelled Wade before being whistled for his fifth foul and taking a seat. Embiid ultimately wasn't needed for any of the fourth period and only logged 25 minutes.

Embiid said postgame that he was not available to return to the action in the fourth and has been feeling his plantar fasciitis on the court. The seven-time All-Star's health is clearly not close to 100 percent.

"I was on a minutes restriction," he said. "(The foot) has been going like that for the past few weeks. It's painful. Just got to manage it.

"If it flares up pretty bad, got to take a step back. We've been able to manage it, but it has its ups and downs."

The Sixers' lead got as low as eight points, but Chicago did not shoot well enough from three-point range (26.3 percent) or have sufficient firepower to sustain a meaningful comeback run. The bottom line is the Sixers are in a different class than the Bulls, who fell to 1-6.

As for the Sixers, they'll wake up Tuesday morning at the top of the Eastern Conference standings.

"You've got some of these nights where you kind of call them professional wins. …You look at the records and all that kind of stuff, but they have a lot of talent," Nurse said. "A lot of these teams play a way that's a little scary. It's fast and it's furious, lots of threes. … They're going to keep coming at you, high-possession games. So they're not easy games and I'm glad that we handled it professionally tonight."

#2804. George Kittle is skeptical that Brandon Aiyuk will "get in" the team facility

As 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk attempts to lay the foundation for a return to the NFL, at least one other 49ers player has expressed skepticism about Aiyuk’s plan.

His new agent, Drew Rosenhaus, has said that Aiyuk will report to the 49ers’ facility on Friday. Tight end George Kittle shared his views regarding that strategy on Thursday when asked whether he’s looking forward to seeing Aiyuk.

“Do you think he gets in the facility?” Kittle said, via Grant Cohn of SI.com.

Kittle then was asked if he thinks Aiyuk gets into the facility.

“Plead the fifth,” Kittle said. “Yeah. You can ask [G.M.] John [Lynch]. Or [coach] Kyle [Shanahan]. Don’t ask me. I’m a conspiracy guy.”

“Is there a conspiracy?” Kittle was asked.

Kittle’s skepticism is warranted. The entire situation is strange, and awkward. Aiyuk clearly hopes to trigger his outright release by reporting for work, after months of making it clear he had no intention to do so.

The 49ers are tiptoeing around the situation, for now. Before the team has to do anything, Aiyuk needs to check the various boxes that put the 49ers in a posture that requires a decision.

Of all potential outcomes, the least likely involves Aiyuk being welcomed back onto the roster. Especially since Aiyuk likely has no desire to be on the roster.

He wants his freedom, and he finally — after months of stubbornness — has initiated the process aimed at making that happen. While it’s possible that the 49ers will search for a hardball outcome that involves keeping him from becoming a free agent while avoiding putting him back on the team, the chances of Aiyuk wandering around the locker room and eventually wearing a 49ers’ helmet again seem to be incredibly slim.

#2802. Sacred Heart Academy claims SCC volleyball title over Amity behind JJ Kirck's 31 digs

WEST HAVEN - It is often said that offense wins games and defense wins championships. Sacred Heart Academy senior libero JJ Kirck will attest to that.

Playing spectacular defense, including 31 digs, Kirck was one of the keys in leading the second-seeded Sharks to a 3-2 (21-25, 25-11, 25-19, 23-25, 15-11) victory over top-seeded Amity in the Southern Connecticut Conference championship game at West Haven High on Thursday evening.

"Our team battled all year," Kirck said after being named the tournament's Most Outstanding Player. "We played so many different games this year, and every game besides one, we came out on top. Tonight was just a show of how much we faced adversity."

The Sharks avenged consecutive SCC championship game losses to Amity and improved to 20-1 on the season.

"It means everything to me," Kirck said. "I'm from Amity. I love all these girls across the net. I grew up playing volleyball with all of them. I am so glad my senior year, I was able to get the dub, and I am so glad I was able to do it with my best friends."

Both teams entered the contest without losing a set thus far in the SCC tournament. Amity, which has been in the final every year since 2021 and won two in a row over the Sharks, delivered the first blow in handing Sacred Heart Academy its first set loss of the tournament by riding the play of Calleigh Parkin and Brooke Cichon in the opening set.

The Sharks pushed back and dominated the second set with a 14-point win that saw a kill by Aliya Church put the Sharks ahead 16-8 and her ace extend the lead to 20-9.

"Their defense played really well," Amity coach Seth Davis said after his team fell to 19-3 on the year. "They dig a lot of balls. They pass really well. Once we started passing, we could keep the ball in system and get the ball to Olive (Hubbard)."

The two teams used strong defensive play to split the next two sets as Kirck's defense thwarted Amity in the third set before she closed it out with a pair of service points.

The Spartans would not go quietly, overcoming a 21-19 fourth-set deficit with a pair of kills by SCC Player of the Year Olive Hubbard, a kill by Parkin, and a block by Hubbard to close the set.

"We did our job from the service line," Sacred Heart Academy coach Dave Alexandro said. "Tough serves to the seams, and it kind of took them out of system which allowed us to get some swings on the ball, and then build up a little bit of a lead. With that confidence, swinging well, hitting the ball well, kind of moving them around the court made a big difference."

The turning point came in the fifth set when a block by Paige Nelson gave Sacred Heart Academy a 10-9 lead. The Sharks never looked back as a kill by Church increased the lead to 12-9 before Isabella Pellegrino sealed the championship with a kill.

Bella Spinato was dominant for Sacred Heart Academy with 43 assists. Pellegrino finished with 25 kills, and Church added 16 kills.

Cichon had 16 kills and 17 digs, and Hubbard finished with 15 kills in the loss for Amity.

JJ Kirck, Sacred Heart Academy: The senior libero had 31 digs as her team topped Amity 3-2 to win the SCC title.

"I know wherever I get the ball up, my setter is going to set it perfectly. My job is really easy. All I have to do is get the ball off the floor." - Sacred Heart Academy's JJ Kirck.

SHA 21 25 25 23 15Amity 25 11 19 25 11Records: SHA 20-1; Amity 19-3.
