#2801. Justin Jefferson says his frustration came from "wanting to win"

Vikings receiver Justin Jefferson engaged in a rare display of frustration late in Sunday’s 19-15 loss to the Lions. He also didn’t talk to reporters after the game, possibly to avoid saying things that would underscore what he was feeling in the moment.

Four days later, Jefferson spoke to the media — and the No. 1 topic was his apparent mood during Minnesota’s first loss of the season.

“It was wanting to win,” Jefferson said, via Kevin Seifert of ESPN, “and I felt like we had plenty of opportunities to win that game and we only hurt ourselves in multiple ways. It’s just wanting better from the whole entire offense especially. And I feel like the potential and the guys that we have in that room is something very, very special. I feel like we’re wasting that if not going out there executing the plays that we have called and just not living to that standard that we feel like we live up to every single day.”

Jefferson, who always says all the right things about his quarterbacks, said all the right things about Kyler Murray, whose performance against Detroit largely contributed to the failure to score more than 15 points.

“I feel like Kyler has continued to grow,” Jefferson said. “I feel like Kyler is continuing to get comfortable with this offense. And I feel like the plays that are there are plays that he’s well capable of making. And those are plays that we’ve seen him make multiple times throughout the season, throughout practice and in training camp as well. So we definitely have faith in him. We have confidence in him to go out there and be that guy that we need him and want him to be.

“But all in all, it’s just executing our plays and just coming ready to play. And I feel like once he’s back out there and he gets more comfortable with the system, more comfortable with just throwing to these different type of receivers, then I feel like he’s going to look a lot better in [reporters’] eyes. But we see that every single day. We see that type of Kyler that we need out there on Sundays every single day. So just bringing that on Sundays. We’re behind him. We’re confident in him, so we just going to keep riding with him.”

For now, the Vikings will ride with Carson Wentz, while Murray is out with a hamstring injury. And Wentz has proven that, of all the quarterbacks who have played for the Vikings since Sam Darnold left for Seattle after the 2024 season, Wentz knows how to get the ball to Jefferson.

Soft bench or not, Wentz has an opportunity to stay on the field if he makes the plays that Murray didn’t on Sunday. Plays that, by the time the game was on the line, coach Kevin O’Connell didn’t allow Murray to try to make.

The one moment that resonates from Sunday’s game wasn’t anything Murray did or failed to do. It came from O’Connell’s decision to punt from the Minnesota 33-yard line on fourth and five with 3:01 to play. Given the manner in which the Lions were running the ball, the likelihood of getting another opportunity to score a touchdown was slim.

Put simply, if O’Connell didn’t trust Murray to gain five yards with the game on the line, O’Connell shouldn’t entrust the starting job to Murray.

For now, Murray technically can’t play due to injury. It’s hard not to wonder whether, if Murray had made the plays needed to outscore the Lions on Sunday, he’d be playing on Monday night despite the injured hamstring.

#2800. INTERVIEW: Bayern icon Makaay critiques Yamal and Mbappe's efforts to deny Kane of "deserved" Ballon d'Or

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): Bayern Munich legend Roy Makaay feels Harry Kane not only richly deserved his Ballon d'Or award, but conducted himself with class compared with some of his closer competitors' campaigns in the race for football's top individual accolade.

“I think this year compared to other years there were quite a lot of candidates to win the Ballon d’Or. What I liked is that the one [Kane] who won didn’t advertise for themselves,” the Dutch striker said at a media round attended by Bulinews.com.

“The other two [Lamine Yamal and Kylian Mbappé] were just speaking at press conferences that they should win it. In the end, it’s the journalists who decide who wins it and that’s the way it should be.

“You shouldn’t depend on making advertisements like Yamal and Mbappé. I mean, they are all amazing players, I like watching them both but I think [the Ballon d’Or winner] is up to the voters.

“I think [Kane’s action in the build up] was the best approach. It was really nice to see him win it and in my eyes he deserved it. If you score 73 goals, win the [German] double and get third-place at the World Cup [with England]

“It shows he deserved it. I think it’s nice not only for Bayern but also the Bundesliga to say we have the Ballon d’Or [winner] in our league,” explained the marksman who played over 183 games for the Bavarian giants between 2003-07.

#2799. LSU makes Division I history with bonkers 109-point win

LSU makes Division I history with bonkers 109-point win appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Angel Reese's former outfit, the No. 9 LSU women's basketball team, recorded a program-record 131-22 victory over Texas Southern on Thursday, winning by 109 points. The win meant that LSU broke the record for the largest margin of victory in the history of the program for a second time running, after beating Southeastern Louisiana 129-35 on Monday, per a post on X by ClutchPoints.

The Tigers improved to 2-0 and outscored Texas Southern 106-9 over the final three quarters after taking a 25-13 lead into the second period. The defining stretch was a 40-0 second-quarter run during which LSU forced nine turnovers, held Texas Southern without a field goal on 13 attempts and shot 15-of-20 from the floor, including 5-of-6 from three-point range.

Texas Southern went without a point after a three-pointer with 24 seconds remaining in the first quarter until Jayla Cornelius connected from beyond the arc at the 6:15 mark of the third. LSU forced 38 turnovers, converting them to a whopping 55-0 advantage on points, as a total of seven players scored in double digits.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

LSU were led by Anna Minaeva's game-high 21 points, as she also recorded seven rebounds and three assists. Mikaylah Williams contributed 20 points, six rebounds and five assists, while Jada Williams finished with 18 points, seven assists and two steals.

Williams scored 16 of her 18 points in the second quarter after going scoreless in the first half. ZaKiyah Johnson added 17 points and eight rebounds, while Grace Knox and MiLaysia Fulwiley each scored 15. Fulwiley also recorded six assists, four blocks and five steals, as Lola Lampley was the seventh LSU player to reach double figures with 10 points.

LSU's next scheduled opponent is McNeese State, with the Tigers set to host the team in their next game, while Texas Southern is scheduled to travel to Rice.

#2798. Why Troy Aikman Doesn’t Deserve Sympathy Over His ESPN Contract Situation

Somebody check on Troy Aikman. Maybe send flowers. Start a GoFundMe. Have Joe Buck organize a benefit concert. Apparently, we're supposed to feel sorry for a guy making $18 million a year because his employer hasn't offered him another contract to talk about football. Somewhere in America, somebody is working a double shift, wondering how they're going to pay the mortgage, while we're discussing whether Aikman feels betrayed because Joe Buck got a new deal.

Forgive me if I don't reach for a tissue.

Aikman is finishing a reported five-year, $90 million contract with ESPN. That's $18 million annually. Divide that by 17 Monday Night Football games, and it's roughly $1.06 million per Monday night. Yes, he prepares, travels and works postseason games. That's not his literal game check. But however you divide it, that's a pretty nice living for explaining why somebody missed a tackle.

Meanwhile, Buck reportedly received a six-year, $108 million extension, and Aikman didn't. Now we're hearing about hurt feelings, reported disagreements and whether their 25-season television partnership is headed for divorce. Apparently, Buck is supposed to save his partner. ESPN is supposed to reward loyalty, and the rest of us are supposed to feel terrible that Aikman might have to explore free agency.

The horror. Maybe he'll have to update his LinkedIn profile.

Look, Aikman has had a remarkable life. Three Super Bowl championships, a Hall of Fame career and approximately $55.5 million in NFL playing earnings. Then came 21 seasons at FOX Sports, where he reportedly made around $7.5 million annually toward the end of his run. Then negotiated an extension worth $13.2 million a year before leaving for ESPN. Add his current five-year, $90 million ESPN contract, and the Cowboys and ESPN alone account for more than $145 million.

We don't know exactly what FOX Sports paid him over those two decades, but factor in those years. In addition to endorsements, appearances and business ventures. It's entirely plausible his career gross earnings have approached or exceeded $250 million. That's a quarter of a billion dollars for playing football, talking about football and selling things to people who enjoy football.

I've had Aikman on my radio shows through endorsement relationships. He's made an extraordinary living, and good for him. But let's not confuse a wealthy broadcaster approaching the end of a contract with somebody standing in the unemployment line.

And why exactly is this Joe Buck's problem?

Buck and Aikman have worked together for 25 seasons. That's impressive. So is being married for 25 years. But plenty of couples eventually decide they'd rather watch television in separate rooms. Buck negotiated a terrific deal. Why should he apologize or jeopardize his own future because ESPN hasn't offered Aikman another enormous contract?

Buck isn't his agent, accountant or emotional support broadcaster. He's the play-by-play guy.

Besides, can we stop acting as though we're about to lose Madden and Summerall? Buck and Aikman are fine. Comfortable. Familiar. Like a restaurant you've been visiting for 25 years because you know exactly what you're getting.

Now we're even discussing whether Aikman will call the Super Bowl. Andrew Marchand of The Athletic recently estimated a 95% chance he works ESPN's first Super Bowl and a 5% chance he doesn't. Five percent? Why is there any percentage devoted to this conversation?

He's under contract through the game. It's the Super Bowl, not an optional company picnic where the potato salad might be questionable.

To be fair, Aikman hasn't threatened to skip it. He's indicated he intends to finish his assignments. Good. That's what he's supposed to do.

However, the speculation has become ridiculous. Enough with the fascination over whether Buck and Aikman can remain professional. People are treating this like The Real Housewives of Monday Night Football. Hurt feelings, awkward looks and contract drama. All we're missing is somebody throwing a drink. Did Buck look irritated? Did Aikman roll his eyes? Is somebody getting voted off the island? Give me a break.

These are grown men making $18 million a year. Everybody has worked with somebody they weren't particularly happy with. Most of us didn't get a million dollars to sit next to them for three hours.

I side with ESPN. If the network wants Aikman back, that's its decision. If it wants somebody younger, more energetic or simply different, that's its decision, too. Monday Night Football should be a destination job, not a lifetime appointment.

Sunday Night Football has become the NFL's premier weekly showcase, but ESPN has an opportunity to make Monday nights special again.

Greg Olsen deserves consideration. Peyton Manning would be fascinating. Pat McAfee would be a gamble, but at least an interesting one. Just don't repeat the Jason Witten experiment by handing the job to somebody because he has a recognizable name. Find somebody who has earned it, can explain the game and makes the broadcast better.

Aikman can negotiate whatever he believes he's worth. ESPN can decide whether to pay it. Buck can enjoy his new contract without being asked to rescue his partner. Nobody is being wronged here. It's business. Sometimes business means somebody decides you're not worth what you think you are.

Troy Aikman spent his playing career winning Super Bowls and his broadcasting career earning enormous checks. He's had one of the great runs in sports. But being successful doesn't make you indispensable, and being disappointed doesn't make you a victim.

So please, save the sympathy. Save the candlelight vigil. Save the GoFundMe.

Don't worry. After nearly four decades of playing football and talking about it, I don't think Aikman will have to start driving for Uber. Unless, of course, Joe Buck needs a ride.

#2797. ‘I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers

'I'm finding my footing': Jaylen Brown's role taking shape with Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown has spent the opening weeks of the season describing the Sixers as a work in progress.

He offered a similarly humble perspective Monday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena. His performance, however, provided the clearest look yet at how he can influence this group while sharing the floor with the club's other established stars.

Brown finished with 25 points, 11 rebounds and five assists in the Sixers' 126-107 win over the Chicago Bulls. He shot 9 for 15 from the field, made four three-pointers and did not commit a turnover.

Five of his rebounds came on the offensive end, an area Brown has emphasized while the Sixers continue piecing together their frontcourt rotation.

"Just a mindset," Brown said. "Just trying to create extra opportunities for my team. As we're trying to patch together our fives and our front line, I try to make an extra contribution and emphasis on the glass."

Brown has also been careful about how he describes his place on a roster that already belonged to Joel Embiid and Tyrese Maxey.

Maxey scored 15 of his 26 points in the fourth quarter Monday, continuing his torrid start to the season. He has led the Sixers in scoring while being the only starter to appear in all eight games.

Brown is not interested in interrupting that.

"Maxey's been leading the way, 100 percent," Brown said. "He's been here. This is his team. Him and Joel have been here for a while, so the mindset has been to complement those guys. I'm finding my footing, but Maxey has been leading the way for sure."

Brown's footing looked firm against Chicago.

Beyond his scoring, he helped the Sixers control the glass, moved the ball within an offense that generated 23 assists during the first half and supplied the physicality Nick Nurse expects from him.

"The dude is tough and he wins," Nurse said. "I'll take that all day long. The degree of difficulty on some of the shots he hit was really amazing. I thought he pounded the glass and got some key rebounds. Makes a lot of winning plays. Man, he's a winner."

Brown spent the first 10 years of his career in Boston. He won a championship there, reached another NBA Finals and grew from a 20-year-old prospect into one of the league's premier two-way wings.

Moving into a new system alongside James, Embiid and Maxey was never going to be seamless.

"I mean, he's fitting in along with all of us," James said. "We're getting better and better. It's not going to happen overnight. He spent his first 10 years in a system in Boston, in the city, so it's going to take some adjustment.

"It takes adjustment for him. It's going to take adjustment for the rest of the guys that joined this franchise as well. But tonight was a great step."

James pointed to Brown's "pressure, his shot-making ability, his rebounding ability" and his professionalism.

Brown's early influence has extended beyond his production.

The Sixers entered Monday with one of the NBA's best half-court defenses. Brown has been one of the group's most persistent voices, stressing communication, preparation and attention to opposing players' tendencies.

"That's the part that I care about the most as a group, not individually," Brown said. "Having a mentality night in and night out, respecting your opponent, respecting the game, making sure that we are executing our coverages, we're being clear in our communication and then we're also taking away tendencies on a night-to-night basis.

"Those things I just explained are over 50 percent of the battle."

Brown understands that maintaining those details across an 82-game schedule will require more than reminders.

"A little bit of accountability," he said. "You learn different guys require different things. How you communicate with each guy may be a little different, but accountability for sure and confrontation if need be.

"The offense is going to be what it's going to be, but our defense has got to be non-negotiable. That's something I say all the time. Non-negotiable. You want to be a good team, defense is non-negotiable."

The Sixers showed both sides of their defensive potential Monday.

They built a double-digit lead during the first half, allowed Chicago to score 36 points in the second quarter and then created separation after halftime. Philadelphia held the Bulls to 20 points in the third quarter before pulling away in the fourth.

Brown did not celebrate their ability to raise their intensity after it slipped.

"I don't like that we have that," he said. "It's good that we have shown the potential, but we've got to keep that switch on. It doesn't work like that where you just flip the switch.

"It can catch up to us and it can bite us against a better team or on a night that we're not being as crisp on offense. It can't be a switch just because we're playing a lesser opponent or whatever the case may be. We've got to make sure that mentality is from the beginning to end. Can't skip any steps."

That urgency accompanies considerable optimism.

Brown said the Sixers enjoy playing together. He loves when James grabs a rebound because "he's like a quarterback," capable of launching an outlet pass or immediately pushing the pace.

The group is learning when to attack, when to move the ball and how to avoid hesitating in its effort to play unselfishly.

"It's a lot of talent, man," Brown said. "We like each other, and we're just kind of building that chemistry of how we can get this thing to a well-oiled machine. I think we're taking steps forward every single day."

The Sixers are 7-1 despite Brown believing those steps remain incomplete. They have won close games, erased sizable deficits and received important contributions from different players.

Brown's performance Monday showed another version of how their pieces can fit.

"We still have a long way to go to being a really, really solid, great team," Brown said. "But we've been able to win some games. We started out the season great, 7-1, and you haven't seen our best version yet.

#2796. NBA rumors: Could Pelicans emerge as Domantas Sabonis trade suitor?

NBA rumors: Could Pelicans emerge as Domantas Sabonis trade suitor? appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The New Orleans Pelicans have rebounded from a poor start to the 2026-27 season, as they have now won four consecutive games to claw back to the .500 mark (4-4). New head coach Jamahl Mosley appears to have stumbled upon a formula that works, balancing out a roster filled with talent but just couldn't seem to put it all together.

With the Pelicans being better than expected in the early goings of this new campaign, perhaps they would be inclined to pull off a consolidation trade to try and upgrade the roster. One potential target that has come up for New Orleans is Sacramento Kings center Domantas Sabonis, who should be available on the trade market yet again with Sacramento headed for yet another losing season.

"The Pelicans, for example, they have the salaries and type of players that the Kings would want where you could give up a Jordan Poole, a $34 million expiring salary. Maybe you can give up a future first-round pick and unload someone else. Maybe it's a three-way or a four-way deal where you can give up Dejounte Murray in that case," Siegel said on the latest episode of the Clutch Scoops show.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"There's a lot of room for the Pelicans here to either go after Sabonis or another big man on the market."

It's not quite clear though, from a basketball perspective, as to why the Pelicans would trade for Sabonis. Zion Williamson remains entrenched as the starter at the four, and New Orleans needs size and rim protection at the five.

This explains why New Orleans went with Yves Missi as their starting center to begin the campaign, and they've now called upon Karlo Matkovic, a floor-spacing shot-blocker, to start with Missi out injured.

Sabonis' presence could also stunt the development of Derik Queen, the man the Pelicans traded a valuable first-round pick for to get in the 2025 NBA Draft. Suffice to say, the Pelicans might be better off looking elsewhere if they were to pull off a trade — perhaps involving Murray, so that they could fully turn over the point guard position to Jeremiah Fears.

#2794. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.

Las Vegas Raiders (4-4) at San Francisco 49ers (6-1): Go 49ers! While this would hurt said tiebreaker, we are rooting for the NFC over the AFC team here. | FOX

Tampa Bay Buccaneers (3-5) at Chicago Bears (6-2): Go Bears! Once again we are back at strength of schedule. | NBC

Minnesota Vikings (6-1) at Buffalo Bills (6-1): Go Vikings! In a week of many easy picks, this might be the easiest. If the Patriots want to defend their AFC East crown, they need the Bills to lose some non-head-to-head games. #2785. Colts reportedly still hoping for ‘Christian McCaffrey-esque’ trade package for Jonathan Taylor

According to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport, the Indianapolis Colts remain at a standstill regarding trading their star running back Jonathan Taylor.

The Colts are hoping for a 'Christian McCaffrey-esque' trade capital return to actually move Taylor, which for historical reference, was between the Carolina Panthers and San Francisco 49ers on October 20, 2022, and netted the Panthers the 49ers' following picks: 2023 2nd round, 2o23 third round, 2023 forth round, and 2024 5th round pick:

"Where we're at is essentially where we've been, which is several teams interested in Taylor," said Rapoport on GMFB on Thursday morning.

"Willing to offer a significant pick package, but maybe not quite what Ed Dodds, who is running personnel now in place of Chris Ballard, who was fired, wants, and what the organization wants."

"They want a Christian McCaffrey type package for Jonathan Taylor. It's clear they don't have it yet. Obviously, they would've already made the trade."

"The other part of this thing is, this is not really someone they want to trade. This is someone they believe is a big part of their future. They'd like to build around him. So it would take a big package to get the Colts to move on from him."

There's been some school of thought that the Colts could even be coveting a first round pick to actually part with Taylor, who team owner and CEO Carlie Irsay-Gordon potentially considers a major cornerstone of the recently jumpstarted rebuild—regardless of who the next general manager is for the franchise.

That would make a great deal of sense, given that Indianapolis is without its 2027 first round pick, which unfortunately looks like a possible Top 5 pick now and belongs to the New York Jets due to last year's trade deadline Sauce Gardner deal.

At any rate, it doesn't appear as though the Colts ideal asking price has been quite met . . . yet. However, with Indianapolis reportedly holding out until closer to the trade deadline early next week to maximize Taylor's trade return, it's possible a very interested suitor could potentially cave and meet their significant asking price to win the Taylor trade sweepstakes over competitors.

To me, some of this seems like some posturing by the Colts though—although I applaud them for holding out hope of maximizing Taylor trade return until closer to the trade deadline, given they arguably didn't get full trade value return on either DeForest Buckner and Quenton Nelson earlier last week.

Taylor has been one of the Colts best running backs in franchise history, as he's second to only franchise leader Edgerrin James in rushing yards (2,208) and carries (1,699) but is the Horseshoe's all-time leader in rushing touchdowns with 77 respectively.

That being said, he turns 28-years-old in January, and while he's been a great player for the franchise, remains a strong Ring of Honor candidate, and is on a Hall of Fame track, NFL running backs typically do not age gracefully into their early 30's—meaning Taylor may only have 2-3 elite years left. That's realistically the same amount of time it takes to complete a successful rebuild for a franchise—if all actually goes according to plan.

For what it's worth, Rapoport also noted that the Colts are holding out hope for either a 3rd or 4th round pick for starting slot wide receiver Josh Downs, who's a pending free agent that requested a trade from Indianapolis earlier last week and has drawn interest from at least a handful of wideout needy teams.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2784. Sayvia Sellers has 17 points and 7 assists, No. 16 Washington women beat Pacific 75-54

SEATTLE (AP) — Sayvia Sellers had 17 points and seven assists, Avery Howell scored 12 of her 15 points in the second half, and No. 16 Washington pulled away in the fourth quarter to beat Pacific 75-54 on Thursday night.

Brynn McGaughy and Devin Coppinger each added 11 points for Washington (2-0).

The Huskies had 16 steals, five by Coppinger, and scored 21 points off 21 Pacific turnovers.

Jordan Dyer led the Tigers with 12 points and JoJo Nworie added 11 points and eight rebounds.

Coppinger gave Washington the lead for good with layup before Howell, Sellers and Amayah Garcia hit consecutive 3-pointers to cap a 12-0 run that made it 32-22 with 1:19 left in the second quarter.

Pacific (1-1) scored eight of the first 10 second-half points, capped when JoJo Nworie hit a 3-pointer that made it a two-point game but the Tigers got no closer.

Both teams shot below 38% from the field, but Washington outscored the Tigers 27-8 from the free-throw line.

Washington: Hosts Cal State Bakersfield next Thursday.

#2783. Joe Milton on his first start: The NFL is about being ready when your number is called

Joe Milton has never started an NFL game. The closest he came was in Week 18 of the 2024 season when Patriots starter Drake Maye played only three snaps before giving way to Milton, who played the final 61 snaps.

Milton looked like a starting NFL quarterback in that game, completing 22 of 29 passes for 241 yards and a touchdown.

Less than two years later, Milton is on his third team and has played only 51 snaps in four appearances with Dallas since.

He will make his first career start on Sunday when the Eagles play the Giants.

"I was just pretty much going through the plays, going through the week like I was a starter, or like I was playing, and just being ready," Milton said Thursday, via Olivia Reiner of the Philadelphia Inquirer. "And that's what the NFL is about: Just being ready when your name is called, and just preparing for those moments."

The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16 after they placed Jalen Hurts on injured reserve due to blood clots. Tanner McKee started the past three games, but went 0-3 as the Eagles ran their losing streak to six games.

Milton has had a crash course in learning the playbook and the terminology, which he said reminds him of the West Coast scheme he had in New England his rookie season.

Milton was asked how much of the Eagles' playbook he knows.

"Shoot, whatever's in this week," he said with a laugh.

#2782. Which Cincinnati teams won Nov. 5 girls volleyball regional semis?

The Ohio High School Athletic Association girls volleyball tournament is heating up, with just eight teams remaining in each of the seven divisions after regional semifinal matches concluded on Nov. 5.

Entering the regional semifinals, 15 Cincinnati girls volleyball teams remained alive across five OHSAA divisions. Two defending champions advanced on Nov. 4, with eight games remaining on Nov. 5. After intense battles on the court, eight Greater Cincinnati teams are moving on to the regional championship matches.

Take a look at which teams will battle for a regional championship and the right to play in the state tournament.

Undefeated Seton had already punched its ticket to the regional finals. On Nov. 5, both St. Ursula and Ursuline Academy swept their way through the semifinals and set up a massive showdown in the regional championship.

St. Ursula defeated Olentangy Orange in straight sets, 25-18, 25-19, 25-21, while Ursuline defeated Dublin Coffman in three, 25-19, 25-23, 25-23. The Bulldogs' and Lions' senior classes have grown accustomed to this high level of success: the two teams have now combined for six regional final appearances over the past four seasons.

The upcoming Region 3 championship at Springfield becomes a rubber match in an intense conference rivalry. St. Ursula (17-8) and Ursuline (18-7) split the regular-season series this year, with each team winning in the other's gym. Now, the second- and third-ranked teams in Division I face off at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, with a trip to the state finals on the line.

The Kings Knights (21-3) entered Butler High School on Nov. 5 with one mission: vanquish the memories of a four-set loss to Dublin Scioto in the 2025 regional semifinals.

Behind a dominant attack scheme and an aggressive serve, they took down Harrison (22-3) 25-19, 25-19, 25-15 to advance to Saturday's Division II, Region 8 final.

"I love playing against good competition. We scouted them and knew their strengths and weaknesses and I thought we did a really good job of balancing things out," Kings head coach Amanda Meadows said.

Since their state championship season of 2023, Kings has worn shirts with the phrase, "All gas. No Brakes." During their current 17-match winning streak, they have demonstrated the ability to pull away from teams at any point in the set.

"They did a couple things that we didn't handle as well. We couldn't keep our serve. They serve-receive at a high rate. We didn't serve enough and they served tough," Harrison head coach Phil Deak said.

With the first set tied 10-10, the Knights rattled off four straight points, keeping the Wildcats at arm's length the rest of the way. In the second set, they went to work at the service line. Laura Raines rattled off seven straight points for a 8-4 lead before Kennady Horsley served out the final five points of the set. The third set was a total takeover as they expanded a 3-2 lead to 9-3, eventually gaining an eight-point advantage at 16-8.

As she has done all year, freshman Sammi Wehrman led the way with 23 kills on a .524 hitting percentage. Raines was second in the category with nine terminations, as the Knights hit .398 as a team.

"She's amazing. I know that in games where I struggle, she definitely steps up and vice versa. We balance off each other really well in service," Raines said.

Senior setter Anabelle Seni had 22 assists to go with three blocks while her protege, freshman Dri Naranjo, had 14 assists and 10 digs.

"They are so different and honestly, I've never run a four-two in my life, but when you look at our dynamics, I don't do things the same way," Meadows said. "Dri's strength, in addition to having great hands, is defense. The girl is an amazing defensive player and having reactions like that obviously comes in hand as a setter. Anabelle is a great setter, great leader, but literally the best blocking setter I've ever coached in my life."

Behind seniors Maddie Ward, Lucy Longworth, Kenna Farris and Stella Whitis, the Wildcats tied the program mark for most wins in a season. They started 10-0 with a win over Taylor and took down Wyoming, Cincinnati Hills Christian Academy and Little Miami in the regular season before beating Anderson for a second time to win a district title.

Farris set the program's single-season record for assists along the way.

"They're a special group of girls. Anytime anything happens for the team, it's because they have good attitudes, they work hard, they like being around each other and they're fun kids," Deak said. "Being in the gym never seemed like it was work. It seemed like it was an opportunity to be together."

The DII, Region 8 final is 2 p.m. Nov. 7 at Butler High School.

The Cougars only needed one win for a district title and a rematch with Anthony Wayne, who swept MND in the 2025 DII state semifinals.

The match that unfolded was truly worthy of a state championship state as the Generals took down the Cougars 25-19, 17-25, 25-19, 22-25, 22-20.

"I thought they did a great job of adjusting against us and it's kind of a game of readjusting to everything," MND head coach Dani Szczepanski said. "It was great volleyball to watch and I could not ask for a better game to be a part of."

The first set was a sign of things to come as Wayne took an early lead at 13-9, but the Cougars just kept hanging around behind attacks from Leah Seeger and Dylan Kozma.

MND responded in the second set, taking advantage of the Generals' deep back line with short, diving serves and an attack that constantly found the floor in zone six.

While the Cougars didn't win the third set, they mounted a comeback from 10 points down to get within four points at 23-19. Their bugaboo in the set was too many attacks that sailed long, a problem that also plagued them in the first stanza. Anthony Wayne's front line of Leah Ruhe, Charley Gardner, Olive Baker and Addelyn O'Shea also feasted on any ball within reach.

"It was a very up-and-down match for us. When we were really high, we were really high. Then when we were really low, we let them get really high, which made us go lower. Our word for the year was 'resilient,' and I thought our girls did a great job of being that during the game," Szczepanski said.

The final two sets took a turn for the dramatic, the tightest two of the match by far. Down 4-1 early in the fourth, MND worked its way back to tie it at 8-8. After the Generals surged to a 15-11 lead, kills from Reagin Rozier, Aubrey McDaniel and Seeger, plus two Grace Shafer aces, tied the match at 17-17. It would remain tied at every point going forward until a Rozier block, a Wayne attack error and a kill from McDaniel sent it to a dramatic fifth set.

After 10 points, it appeared the Cougars had seized all the momentum as two kills and a block from Kendall Hubbard propelled them to a 7-3 lead. Anthony Wayne responded with a 9-2 run out of a timeout to take a 12-9 advantage. After Szczepanski burned her final timeout, kills from Seeger, Kozma and McDaniel gave MND a 13-12 lead.

From there, neither team had a two-point advantage until the final margin. Both teams had four match points, the first three on the Cougars' side, then four of the next five in favor of the Generals.

With the deciding set tied at 20, MND committed an attack error before Hubbard was called for lift to bring the marathon to its finish line.

"We didn't have the best record in the season, had some adversity to fight through and had some injuries. We were trying to find our footing a lot and I feel like that's how we were tonight. When we were looking for our footing, it was hard to find, but once we found it, we were climbing super high," Szczepanski said.

The Cougars close the season at 12-11 and graduate six seniors: Hubbard, Shafer, Seeger, McDaniel, Kozma and Cami Reisert.

"We are going to miss them greatly, not only from a volleyball aspect but from a leadership aspect and who they are as human beings. That's going to be a big loss for our program but I'm really proud of them. They stepped up in many different ways this year," Szczepanski said.

After two sets, Fenwick found its back against the wall. Wyoming had won each by narrow margins, but time was running out on Fenwick's chances for a third consecutive trip to the regional finals. The Falcons responded in force, completing the reverse sweep with a 23-25, 22-25, 25-20, 25-20, 15-12 victory against the Cowboys.

Wyoming becomes just the second team to take Fenwick to five sets, with McNicholas doing so in the regular season. The Cowboys' season comes to an end at 18-6. Wyoming will graduate five seniors: Sarah Mouch, Athina Rickert, Phoebe Alvis, Emily Streets and Morgan Thomas.

Taylor blasted Johnstown in the opening set, and the Yellowjackets never looked back. Taylor advances to the regional finals with a 25-11, 25-18, 25-9 win over the Johnnies in the semifinals. The Yellowjackets have never reached the state finals before and still face a final significant hurdle against Greater Catholic League-Coed co-champion Fenwick.

Fenwick (25-2) takes on Taylor (24-2) in the Division V, Region 15 championship game at Lakota East. The match will be played at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7.

Madeira is one game away from ending a 45-year state tournament drought after a 25-19, 25-21, 25-15 sweep against Arcanum. The Amazons haven't reached the Final Four since 1981, when they finished as state runners-up. The only thing standing in their way is a revenge match against Versailles, which handed Madeira a defeat in the 2025 district championship match. Versailles eliminated Milford Center Fairbanks in three sets to set up the grudge match.

Madeira (20-6) will face Versailles (20-6) in the regional championship at 2 p.m. on Saturday, Nov. 7, at Kettering Fairmont.

This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Which Cincinnati teams won Nov. 5 girls volleyball regional semis?

#2780. LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it

LeBron James is having fun in Philly and one fan's wine can prove it originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James is one of kind in so many ways. What he did Monday night might be the cherry on top.

In the third quarter of the Sixers' 126-107 victory over the Bulls, James decided he'd take fan interaction to a whole new level.

James looked for a transition pass from Tyrese Maxey, who snagged a rebound. James was behind the pass and his momentum took him into the stands, where he disappeared for a few moments.

It wasn't until another angle was shown on replay that we could see what actually went down in the darkness of the seats. James didn't hold back giving reporters a play-by-play.

"I spilled some of his wine," he said postgame. "I crashed into him, spilled some of his wine. So I wanted to see if it was worth it on my arm and how good it was. It was alright."

Troy Clemons – the Sixers fan whose drink spilled – explained his perspective on the interaction with James to Kris Rhim of ESPN.

"[I was] walking to my seat … LeBron crashed into me, he grabbed me, he said 'My bad,' my drink spilled everywhere. He took my drink from me and he basically just took a sip of it, gave it back and ran back on the [court]."

"At the end of the day he's another man like me," Clemons said. "I mean he's LeBron, but at the end of the day, I threw that cup in the trash. And it's nothing up with him, I just got a fresh drink though."

It should be noted that players are not supposed to drink from fans' cups, but it's rare for the NBA to fine someone for a spontaneous interaction such as this. In 2019, a similar thing happened when Mike Scott tumbled into the Bucks' court-side seats. The Sixers star jokingly took a sip of a fan's beverage and the NBA opted not to fine him. Time will tell if James has the same fate.

If nothing else, an interaction like this just shows how much fun James seems to be having.

The 24-year veteran noted the importance of finding joy in the game as he grinds through the season.

"It's a long marathon. And if you don't find ways to have fun throughout it, even though you're working and even though you're locked in, then it could wear on you. So for me, especially at this point in my career, being able to just appreciate the moments, the small moments, these fans are great to us. We try to be great to them as well. By playing the game the right way, playing the game hard and then also just being showmen as well, it was a great game for us."

Head coach Nick Nurse also weighed in on the 41-year-old's mindset coming into the season.

"This summer I think he probably stated publicly that he wanted to go some place and have some fun playing. He loves the game, [it's his] 24th year. It's a group of guys, like I keep saying, that have been super focused in the intent of playing the right way. I think he's a big part of that."

The vibes are high. It's difficult for them not to be.

With Monday's win against the Bulls, the Sixers are 7-1 and lead the Eastern Conference. They've won four in a row and the only team they've lost to is the Pistons. The Sixers will face them on Wednesday to start a three-game road trip.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2779. 'We can laugh about it.' Aaron Donald really doesn't want to bumble another fumble

Since coming out of retirement before this season, Rams defensive lineman Aaron Donald adopted a humble approach, always allowing younger, less-accomplished players to go ahead of him during drills.

But on Thursday, the future Hall of Famer enthusiastically moved to the front of the line.

The Rams practiced scooping fumbled footballs off the turf.

"I needed that," Donald said Thursday, chuckling. "Learn bending and getting down."

Last Sunday against the Chargers, Donald missed the opportunity to score his first NFL touchdown when, with a clear shot to the end zone, he failed to come up with a fumble, albeit in hilarious fashion.

"I saw what he was thinking because it's going right in front of you and he's thinking, 'I'm going to scoop and score,'" coach Sean McVay said after the game. "Then he knocks that ... out of bounds. I mean, what the hell?

"His face, he couldn't even look at me and make eye contact after that. But at least we can laugh about it right now."

Donald was still laughing about it Thursday.

"I should have slowed down, but I felt people behind me and I'm like, 'I'm going to get hit in my back. That's going to hurt,'" he said. "So I tried to go full speed and scoop it. I thought the ball was going to bounce this way but it bounced that way, and it just looked all damn silly.

"Then I hit the ball out. I just laid down. 'I can't believe I just did that.'"

Donald was grinning throughout his session with reporters while replaying the missed opportunity — and the new opportunity on the horizon with edge rusher Myles Garrett back at practice.

Read more: Sean McVay's unusual kicker experiment leaves Rams with little margin for error

McVay has not said whether Garrett will be activated from injured reserve and play for the first time since undergoing knee surgery after the season opener.

But Donald sounded as if that could happen Sunday when the Rams play the Washington Commanders in Landover, Md.

Donald did not play in the opener, so whenever Garrett returns would mark the first time the future Hall of Famers will play together.

"If Myles is 100% ready to go, and plays this week, that's going to be amazing for us," Donald said. "It's always good when you have all the pieces of the puzzle."

Donald, a three-time NFL defensive player of the year, and Garrett, a two-time winner, have a combined 236½ sacks. Last season with the Cleveland Browns, Garrett set an NFL season record with 23 sacks.

Donald has played in six games for a team that opened the season as the Super Bowl favorite but is 4-4 after its win over the Chargers.

Read more: NFL Week 9 picks: Bills prevail over Vikings; Packers beat Patriots

Donald does not have a sack, but he has 12 tackles, three for losses.

Will Garrett's return free Donald from double teams and other schemes?

"I'm pretty sure," Donald said. "The guy did have 20-something sacks last year."

Donald said the season has not gone as expected, but he is enjoying it.

"I'm enjoying the process, I'm enjoying myself week in and week out getting better, enjoying being out there with the guys," he said. "And just playing this game."

The Rams activated kicker Joshua Karty to the roster from the practice squad and signed cornerback David Long to the practice squad. The Rams selected Long in the third round of the 2019 draft and he played three-plus seasons before stints with four other teams. ... For the second day in a row, cornerback Trent McDuffie (back), tight end Colby Parkinson (ribs) and safety Kam Curl (foot) did not practice.

#2778. No. 11 UCLA's defense leads the way in 78-42 victory over UC Irvine

The No. 11 UCLA Bruins looked a bit sharper in their second game of the season, knocking off UC Irvine 78-42 on Thursday to move to 2-0 on the young season.

After battling in the first half in the season opener against Lehigh, the Bruins got off to a much better start this time around. UCLA led 23-8 after the first quarter, as the team didn't allow the lesser-talented Anteaters to stick around.

UCLA finished strong too, pitching a shutout in the fourth quarter. The Bruins didn't allow a single fourth quarter point, which is a good sign for a new-look Cori Close defense that no longer has Lauren Betts roaming the paint. Four different Bruins recorded a block, as rim protection will likely be a group effort this season.

KK Bransford led UCLA with four steals and freshman Somto Okafor looks like a very promising defender on the wing for UCLA. Okafor notched three steals in 23 minutes off the bench on Thursday. Okafor grabbed three steals in the opener on Monday beginning her UCLA career with back-to-back three steal games.

UCLA had four scorers in double-figures but it was sophomore forward Sienna Betts leading the team with 17 points on 8-10 shooting in just 21 minutes. Junior guard Elina Aarnisalo added 14 points with five rebounds and five assists but did turn the ball over six times.

Timea Gardiner continues to look strong in her return to action. Gardiner missed all of last season but the forward scored 12 points, grabbed five rebounds and knocked down a pair of threes. The three point line was still a battle for UCLA though, shooting 25% on 24 three point attempts.

It was an improved showing for the national champions, with UCLA displaying an elite defense that will be crucial to their success this season. UCLA will have a full week before their next game, as they'll host Arizona next Thursday.

#2777. Kon Knueppel injury update: Hornets reveal forward has hamstring strain

The Charlotte Hornets seemingly got the exact news they did not want.

Not even six minutes into his regular season debut, Rookie of the Year runner up Kon Knueppel left a game Wednesday, Nov. 4 against the Brooklyn Nets, with what the team then called left leg soreness.

On the latest injury report that published Thursday, Nov. 5, however, the team confirmed that it was a left hamstring strain, which was the same injury that sidelined him for the season's first eight games. The Hornets also already declared Knueppel out for their game Friday, Nov. 6 against the Boston Celtics.

The injury took place midway through the first quarter of Charlotte's game against Brooklyn.

Although there didn't appear to be a specific play in which Knueppel got hurt, he exited shortly after he dribbled the ball off his foot. During the play, Knueppel tried to dribble the ball behind his back but it caromed off his right leg, forcing him to take a small step as he tried to recover it. Immediately after the play, Knueppel limped but tried to stay in the game before quickly heading to the bench. He eventually went into the locker room and did not return.

After the game, Hornets coach Charles Lee told the local broadcast that he did not have an update on Knueppel and said he would be evaluated once the team returned to Charlotte later Wednesday.

Knueppel scored 3 points, all from free throws, missed his only shot attempt – a 3-pointer – and committed 2 turnovers in 5:51 on the floor against the Nets. He did not record any other statistic.

"Hopefully I can contribute in a capacity similar to last year, and with all the offseason work that I put in, even more," Knueppel told the Hornets broadcast in a pregame interview. "It might take me a little bit to get to that full capability, but tonight I'll be ready to go and help us get a win."

Last season, his first in the NBA after the Hornets selected him with the No. 4 overall pick in the 2025 NBA Draft, Knueppel recorded 18.5 points, 5.3 rebounds and 3.4 assists per game. He converted 3-pointers at a 42.5% clip and lost out to his college teammate at Duke, Cooper Flagg, for the Rookie of the Year honor.

#2775. Colts reportedly have received enticing trade offer for remaining starting veteran defensive tackle

According to The 33rd Team NFL Insider Ari Meirov, the Indianapolis Colts have received a better trade offer for veteran starting nose tackle Grover Stewart than they did for his former interior teammate DeForest Buckner:

For reference, Buckner was traded to the San Francisco 49ers early last week for their 2027 4th round pick, which presumably means that Indianapolis can either acquire a 4th round pick that's from an NFL suitor worse than the 49ers (6-1), or perhaps, although it would be surprising, there could be a potential 3rd round pick.

I would realistically heavily lean much towards the former, given Stewart's age, play, position, and pending free agency.

While it's arguable that the Colts failed to maximize Buckner's true trade value (*especially in light of the ensuing Jeffery Simmons trade), by doing him and his family a solid by dealing him back to the franchise that drafted him as a first rounder back in 2016, and with whom he played his first four seasons for prior to being traded to Indianapolis, it would still be surprising to see Stewart garner more draft compensation, all things considered.

As a looming free agent at 33-years-old, Stewart is your typical one dimensional run stuffing nose tackle, and has had a bit of a down season for his normally high standards. That being said, it's highly possible that a change of scenery to a playoff hopeful and defensive tackle needy team could bring out some vintage 'Big Grove' out of the gates for the season's final stretch run.

On the season, Stewart has 26 tackles (8 solo), 2 tackles for loss, 0.5 sacks, and a pass defensed during all 8 starts during the 2026 campaign.

Stewart has long been a personal favorite of Colts team owner and CEO Carlie Irsay-Gordon, much like star running back Jonathan Taylor remains for that matter—which could make him especially difficult to part with on a personal level and regarding his longstanding veteran leadership in the Indianapolis locker room.

That being said, if they could get a 3rd or 4th round pick for Stewart, then that's a move the franchise should make, as he's getting a bit long in the tooth at defensive tackle and doesn't factor into the team's long-term plans, especially with an ongoing rebuild realistically taking at least the next 2-3 seasons—if all goes according to plan.

#2773. 2026-27 NHL season predictions: Expert picks for Stanley Cup playoffs, champion and major awards this season

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Following a brief offseason and an expedited preseason, the 2026-27 NHL season has arrived. Before the puck drops on opening night, we have some predictions to make.

If this season feel like it's staring earlier than usual, that's because it is. The NHL is adopting an 84-game regular season, which just means more hockey for the rest of us. That also means a long and arduous journey to the Stanley Cup is ever so slightly more difficult.

So, what will that journey look like? The Carolina Hurricanes are looking to defend their title as Cup champions, but they'll have to contend with a deep Eastern Conference and some real juggernauts in the Western Conference. With that in mind, we logged our expert predictions for the playoff field in each conference -- and there were a few surprises -- before making our Stanley Cup Final selections.

We also called our shot on the winners of each major individual award. Connor McDavid is looking for his fourth Hart Trophy, but he's got some competition from an up-and-coming superstar in his own division. Races for the Norris Trophy and Vezina Trophy should be pretty wide open, and unexpected candidates always emerge throughout the season.

It should be a fun season in the NHL, so follow along with all our coverage at CBS Sports. Let's get things kicked off with the season preview and predictions, which will of course be 100% accurate.

Baumgartner: I don't see many new faces entering the conversation when it comes to the Eastern Conference playoff race. A healthy Florida Panthers side will slot themselves right back towards the top of the Atlantic, and a revamped Washington Capitals side should be able to make some noise in the Metropolitan. Besides that, I have six 2026 playoff teams returning to the dance in 2027.

Nivison: As much as I hate to go with chalk in preseason predictions -- because it rarely happens that way -- there's no denying the top dogs in the West. The Avalanche and Stars are the class of the Central Division, and the Golden Knights should pound a pitiful Pacific Division into a pulp. Things get more interesting behind those clear Cup contenders, and I'm projecting another significant leap forward from the Mammoth. I also think Macklin Celebrini will be successful in dragging the Sharks to the playoffs in his third NHL season.

Nivison -- Columbus Blue Jackets: The Blue Jackets have been agonizingly close to the playoffs in each of the last two seasons, and I think they get over the hump in 2026-27. There are still questions in Columbus. Can Adam Fantilli be a franchise player at center? Is the forward group as a whole good enough to keep pace in a competitive Eastern Conference? Still, I am sure the Blue Jackets have a really strong blue line, led by Norris Trophy winner Zach Werenski, and goaltender Jet Greaves is on track to become a Vezina Trophy candidate. Internal improvements from the young core will then get Columbus above the cut line.

Baumgartner -- San Jose Sharks: The Sharks narrowly missed out on the 2026 Stanley Cup Playoffs, finishing just four points short of the second wild card. San Jose added a good group of veterans over the summer, but the most important offseason move was selecting Ivar Stenberg with the No. 2 pick in the Draft. Stenberg joins the youth movement in San Jose, which is spearheaded by new captain Macklin Celebrini, who broke the Sharks single-season points record with 115 last season. The Sharks are a better team than they were last year, and with Celebrini entering his third season as a legit Hart Trophy contender, this team won't be fun to play against.

Nivison: Yes, the Mammoth have a brutal path coming out of the Central Divison, but just close your eyes and imagine this scenario with me. Logan Cooley becomes a superstar center. Dylan Guenther hits 50 goals. Dmitry Simashev asserts himself as a budding top-four defenseman. Anders Lee and Vincent Trocheck provide necessary veteran depth. Now open your eyes. Utah is your Western Conference champion.

Unfortunately for the Mammoth, they will run into a refreshed Panthers team that now has double-barreled Tkachuks. Last season, we found out that all it takes to stop Florida is to hope all of its key players miss significant time with injury. The long offseason was probably a blessing in disguise as it allowed the Panthers to get healthy, and that's bad news for everyone else. The structure in front of the crease will limit some of the volatility we saw from Jacob Markstrom in New Jersey, and Florida wins its third Stanley Cup in four years.

Baumgartner: Before last season, the Carolina Hurricanes' kryptonite was the Eastern Conference Final. The Hurricanes were able to overcome their demons and win the East before ultimately taking home the franchise's first Stanley Cup since 2006. In the West, the Dallas Stars have been running into the same problem. After three straight seasons ending in the Western Conference Final, the Stars regressed last postseason, flaming out in the first-round to the Minnesota Wild. The Central Division will once again be a gauntlet, but I'm ready for Dallas to take the next step and reach the Stanley Cup Final.

While Dallas has continued to fall short in recent years, the Panthers have done the opposite. The Panthers made three consecutive Stanley Cup Final appearances, winning back-to-back Cups in 2023-24 and 2024-25. Last season, Florida's three-peat attempt came to a halt on day one of training after captain Aleksander Barkov went down with a non-contact injury, tearing both the ACL and MCL in his right knee. An offseason of healing for Barkov, and the rest of the banged up Panthers, has the team refreshed entering September for the first time in years. With nearly the entire 2025 Stanley Cup winning core still on the team and Brady Tkachuk added to the fold, Florida looks ready to win its third Stanley Cup in four seasons.

Nivison: Celebrini should have won the Hart Trophy last season, but for some incomprehensible reason, he wasn't even a finalist. This year, Celebrini replicates that performance and gets San Jose to the postseason to take home his first MVP award at age 20. Hughes posts a career year in his first full season with the Wild, and Spencer Carberry gets his flowers for winning the Metro Division after missing the playoffs last year.

Baumgartner: McDavid's ability to take over a game was evident nearly every time he touched the ice last season. His 24 points (11 goals,13 assists) in the final 14 games of the regular season -- without Leon Draisaitl in the lineup -- was one of the many reasons I put him No. 1 on my Hart Trophy ballot. With just two years left on his current contract, desperation may start to kick in to get the job in Edmonton, which would lead to another MVP-caliber season. Barkov is healthy and the frontrunner to win his fourth Selke, and Dahlin will have another strong season to take home his first career Norris Trophy.

#2772. NFL trade deadline: 49ers GM explains Mac Jones trade price

The San Francisco 49ers aren't actively looking to move backup quarterback Mac Jones, and general manager John Lynch made it clear that the team would need a significant offer to consider trading him.

Jones has generated trade speculation throughout the offseason and, with quarterback-needy teams such as the Minnesota Vikings and Philadelphia Eagles potentially looking for options, could be a target ahead of the Nov. 10 deadline.

However, the 49ers have consistently shown little interest in moving Jones without receiving substantial compensation. Appearing on KNBR, Lynch reiterated that San Francisco is comfortable keeping its backup quarterback.

"We'd have to be really motivated to move Mac, a situation where it makes too much sense," Lynch said. "But we're very comfortable with keeping Mac."

That stance makes sense considering what Jones provided the 49ers last season. He went 5-3 as a starter while Brock Purdy dealt with injuries, helping San Francisco reach the playoffs despite significant roster attrition.

Jones proved to be a capable replacement for Purdy, giving the 49ers valuable insurance at the most important position on the field. Reports have indicated that San Francisco would want at least a second-round pick to consider moving him.

With the trade deadline approaching, Lynch's comments suggest that hasn't changed. San Francisco would need an offer it simply couldn't refuse to part with Jones; otherwise, the team appears content to keep one of the NFL's more capable backup quarterbacks behind Purdy.

#2769. Lakers star Luka Doncic joins LeBron James, issues strong cease and desist

Former Lakers teammates LeBron James and Luka Dončić are fighting a common opponent off of the court.

James is looking to get an AI sports betting app, which is named Bron AI, to stop using his likeness without authorization, a spokesperson for James confirmed to The California Post.

LeBron James #23 of the Philadelphia 76ers looks on during the game against the Chicago Bulls on November 2, 2026 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NBAE via Getty Images

Luka Doncic (77) of the Los Angeles Lakers stands for the national anthem before a game against the Portland Trail Blazers on Tuesday, Oct. 27, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

An attorney issued a cease-and-desist letter on the 76ers star's behalf to PlayVision Inc., the sports technology startup that owns Bron AI.

The advertisement for Bron AI that was posted to social media on Wednesday that led to the cease-and-desist shows a person meant to resemble James – wearing a No. 23 basketball jersey with the name "Bron" on it – flipping through the app.

The social media posts amassed over 900,000 views in less than 24 hours before being removed from X/Twitter after Front Office Sports first reported the cease-and-desist.

James, who left the Lakers for the 76ers in free agency this past summer, has an exclusive endorsement deal with Polymarket.

Basketball players Luka Doncic and LeBron James in Lakers jerseys. Los Angeles Times via Getty Images

Adam Mendelsohn, James's longtime media advisor, told The Post that Bron AI's ad is "another example of the decline and lack of originality in sports media."

"It's all about cheap clicks and aggregation," Mendelsohn said in a statement to The Post. "A lot of pundits do it too. I'm sure their intent all along was for us to send a cease and desist in order to play the social media game. You would prefer not to respond but you don't have a choice in a situation like this."

Bron AI's ad featured several athletes, including NBA players Giannis Antetokounmpo, Donovan Mitchell and Dillon Brooks.

A cease-and-desist on behalf of Dončić was also sent to PlayVision Inc., a source close to the Lakers superstar told The Post.

So, not everything is perfect for the Slovenian sharpshooter. Well, on the court it might be.

#2768. Bradshaw Defends Aikman As Money Drives MNF Feud

Terry Bradshaw refuses to accept the nasty rumors swirling around his former broadcast partners. The Hall of Famer knows both men well and sees a different story than the one playing out in headlines. He reached out to Joe Buck directly to clear the air before the Monday night show even aired.

Bradshaw didn't wait for press releases or vague leaks to form his opinion; he went straight to the source. "I talked to Buck via text and told him I don't assume everything I'm reading is correct," Bradshaw said. This direct contact shows the old guard still values their bond over the drama. Bradshaw worked with the duo at Fox Sports for twenty years before they moved to ESPN. He knows their characters better than any reporter sitting in a studio.

The reports claim the split is purely about cash. Bradshaw admits the money angle is the main topic of conversation right now. "(The reporting has been) it's about money," Bradshaw revealed. Buck signed a massive six-year deal worth around $108 million to stay at ESPN. Aikman has been waiting for his own contract talks to finish. The Athletic reported that Aikman's side sought a new deal in the same neighborhood as Buck's and was rebuffed, with ESPN pausing talks until after the Super Bowl. That pay gap seems to be the root of the tension.

Even with the dollar signs flashing, Bradshaw thinks the narrative is wrong. He sees Troy Aikman as a man who would never act out of petty spite. "I find that hard to believe because Troy Aikman is not like that. He's not like that. My understanding right now is things are okay," Bradshaw said. The broadcasts have continued without any visible on-screen issues. The chemistry still works for the viewers even if the business side is messy.

The timing of Buck's deal seems to be the real problem. Bradshaw explained that the two men were supposed to enter ESPN as a package. "I have heard that they were going to go into ESPN together and get a new deal together," Bradshaw added. The fact that Buck got his contract first created a rift where none had existed before. It wasn't a lack of friendship that caused the split, but a lack of coordination.

Greg Olsen has been mentioned as the potential replacement for the booth. If the partnership ends, the 2027 Super Bowl could be their last game together. That is a long way to go for a team that just signed a new deal. Bradshaw's voice carries weight because he has seen these deals before.

At any rate, Taylor is one of the last key remaining longstanding veteran pillars of the Colts prior regime and remains a Top 5 player at the running back position—albeit an elite player who turns 28-years-old in January and realistically may only have 2-3 prime seasons left at a running back position that isn't forgiving for 30-year-olds.

That may be at best, the amount of time that it takes for the Colts to complete an expedited and successful rebuild (if all goes according to plan) given they don't have a first round pick in 2027, still lack a viable franchise quarterback answer, and the current sad state of their diminished roster collectively (thanks, Chris Ballard!).

There's probably some sentiment for Taylor, who's a strong franchise Ring of Honor candidate some day, remains on a Pro Football Hall of Fame track, and trails only Edgerrin James as the franchise's all-time leading rusher in yards and carries, while already holding the record for most rushing touchdowns in Colts history.

It won't be an easy decision to make either way, unless the Colts are blown away, and it appears as though it may take that type of "king's ransom" running back package for Indianapolis to seriously entertain moving Taylor.

#2765. 76ers' Joel Embiid assessed postgame flagrant 1 foul by NBA for kick to Pistons' Cade Cunningham

Philadelphia 76ers center Joel Embiid was assessed a postgame flagrant foul 1 for kicking Cade Cunningham in the first quarter of Wednesday's 112-106 win over the Detroit Pistons, the league announced on Thursday.

The play in question occurred at the 5:32 mark of the first quarter. With Cunningham defending him at the 3-point line, Embiid kicked out with his right leg on an initial shot attempt before passing to LeBron James. His kick struck Cunningham in the groin, causing the Pistons guard to bend over in pain.

Cunningham was slow to recover as Embiid cut past him for a dunk. He also trailed behind as play went to the other side of the court, grabbing the area that was struck. No foul was called on the play.

Whether it was the pain from the kick or perhaps frustration from no call being made, Cunningham only had nine points in the first half before finishing with 26 (shooting 1-for-9 on 3s) and 11 assists.

During Wednesday's game, Embiid was whistled for a technical foul for shoving Detroit's Tolu Smith under the basket with 1:25 remaining in the first half.

By NBA rules, a player assessed a flagrant foul 1 — defined as "unnecessary contact committed by a player against an opponent" — during the regular season receives one point for the infraction. Accumulating six points results in a one-game suspension. Wednesday's flagrant 1 penalty was the first for Embiid this season.

Two flagrant 1 fouls during a game results in ejection. A flagrant 2 comes with an automatic ejection.

Embiid tallied 26 points and 15 rebounds in the Sixers' win, which improved Philadelphia to 8-1 (4-0 on the road). Jaylen Brown led the 76ers with 30 points, adding 7 rebounds.

#2763. Sandia Matadors finish what they started to win Class 5A boys soccer title

ALBUQUERQUE, N.M. (KRQE) – If you considered seeding, you could call the Class 5A state championship boys' game a Cinderella battle. Two high seeds, the Sandia Matadors at number nine and the Capital Jaguars at number seven, decided the champion, with Sandia winning in a blowout at the end, 5-1.

Teo Ojeda scored two goals in the second half to help the Matadors complete their improbable run to the blue trophy. Sandia showed flashes of doing something special back in the quarterfinals when they knocked defending champion and number one seed La Cueva out of the postseason. They followed that up with a semifinal defeat of the Cleveland Storm and refused to be denied in the final.

"I feel like I am on top of the world, in the clouds, what can you see more," said Sandia head coach Ryan Sanchez. "I have always seen this team winning state, and that's never been on the back burner; like, they can win everything that they want. I hoped it had happened the last time; we stumbled at the end, but this time it wasn't going to happen that way."

It's the third boys' soccer championship title in Sandia history and the first since 1992.

#2760. Trevor Connelly continues road trip to remember in Golden Knights’ win over Detroit

DETROIT—In a sense, it felt that every move made in the calendar year of 2026 has been preparing for Trevor Connelly to make his impact.

It explains why the Vegas Golden Knights were not willing to make big splashes at the trade deadline last season, and why they were willing to ship Pavel Dorofeyev off to the New York Rangers. While Connelly had struggled with injuries before, he has been absolutely dynamite at every level prior to this season.

What has taken some aback has been how quickly Connelly has found his groove at the NHL level, and this recent road trip through the Eastern Conference has been a perfect example of the rookie being on his game.

In Boston on Nov. 2, Connelly put together a memorable moment by shaking off a first period hit to force overtime with a third period goal. The next night in Pittsburgh, Connelly would again tie the game late to open the door for Tomas Hertl's eventual game-winner in regulation.

On Thursday, the magic continued for Vegas' latest star attraction, as Connelly and the Golden Knights continued to put together an impressive road swing with a 2-1 victory over the Detroit Red Wings.

It took some time for the excitement to start ramping up, however, as both teams were unable to find any dangerous opportunities to get the scoring going.

While the Golden Knights did get themselves into some trouble with their puck management, goaltender Carter Hart was able to make timely saves and keep Detroit from getting on the scoreboard.

However, the dam would finally break in the third period, as a shot bounced off the end boards and directly on the stick of Anton Johansson for the first goal to fall in Detroit's favor.

Just over two minutes later, the Golden Knights would respond with an excellent 200-foot play involving multiple players. Noah Hanifin was able to clear the puck quickly and gave Vegas' forwards an opportunity to move into the offensive zone, eventually giving Braedan Bowman an opportunity to beat Red Wings goaltender Daniil Tarasov up high to level things again.

Then, with just under two minutes remaining, Connelly made magic happen once again.

Entering the offensive zone with the puck on his stick, the rookie was able to make a nifty move to get past a Red Wings defender, then using another as a screen to beat Tarasov on the far side for his third straight game with a third period goal.

Connelly is now once again tied among all NHL rookies with seven goals (Chicago Blackhawks forward Roman Kantserov) and 12 points (Boston Bruins forward James Hagens).

Hart also put together arguably his best goaltending effort of the season, stopping 25 of 26 shots.

Connelly and the Golden Knights will now get an opportunity to come home on a high when the road trip concludes against the Buffalo Sabres on Sunday.

#2757. Bremen's Füllkrug set for Germany recall alongside Ebnoutalib and Havertz

According to Sky Germany, Jürgen Klopp intends to nominate Niclas Füllkrug, unless anything extraordinary happens.

The pay-TV broadcaster reports that Füllkrug will be included in the squad and given the chance to prove himself under Klopp.

Recently, there had been doubts due to Füllkrug's age (33), which the national team coach even voiced publicly at one stage.

However, the powerful striker's goalscoring record since returning to Werder Bremen seems to have dispelled these concerns for now.

Tomorrow, Thursday around 11:30 CET, Klopp will present his DFB squad for the last two internationals of the 2027 calendar year.

Füllkrug is expected to be one new name included alongside Younes Ebnoutalib who also has a good chance of being nominated again.

The 23-year-old striker from Eintracht Frankfurt has maintained his strong form after his first two international outings.

So far, Ebnoutalib has six Bundesliga goals to his name. Kai Havertz from Arsenal is also likely to be a shoo-in for Klopp's selection.

However, former BVB academy and AS Monaco hopeful Paris Brunner is not expected to receive a phone call from Klopp.
