#2756. Jaguars-Ravens report card shows a workmanlike effort in all categories

Grading the Jacksonville Jaguars 33-10 victory over Baltimore Ravens on a Thursday Night game in Baltimore.

The Jaguars (6-2) will have a long week before their next game, also on the road, against the Tennessee Titans on Nov. 15.

It was a night when a solid, slightly-above performance was just fine. QB Trevor Lawrence fired a couple of big passes to TE Nate Boerkircher and WR Parker Washington to pave the way to a 17-3 halftime lead. It was nice to see RB Bhayshul Tuten running hard after there was doubt concerning his availability and Ameer Abdullah and Chris Rodriguez helped the Jags control the clock in the second half.

Jaguars coach Liam Coen lamented a bit after the game about leaving points on the field and he was right: the Jags had to settle for Cam Little field goals after getting first downs at the Ravens' 21-, 26-, 27- and 23-yard lines. Travis Hunter got careless with the ball on his only touch of the game, fumbling a short pass away and rookie offensive guard Emmanuel Pregnon had three false start penalties.

With 10 days until the next game, there's plenty of time to iron out those issues.

Everyone from Baltimore to Duuval knew what the Ravens were going to do with an undrafted free-agent rookie quarterback making his first NFL start: try to hammer the Jags with Derrick Henry, who has gorged himself at the buffet line disguised as the Jaguars defense in the past when he played for Tennessee.

That was then. This is now. The Jags never let that strategy get off the ground and Henry had only 57 yards on 11 carries, with 6 yards in the second half. The Jaguars played well against the run (until Justice Hill broke off a 65-yard run against a group of substitutes in the fourth quarter) in their first game without nose tackle DaVon Hamilton and got interceptions from Jourdan Lewis and Montaric Brown.

Fun fact: Hill's TD, with 7:15 left in the game, was the Ravens' first first down of the second half. The Ravens converted on 4 of 14 third downs.

Cam Little made four field goals look routine and he's made 34 of his last 36 attempts in his last 17 games. Punter Logan Cooke averaged a season-high 52.0 yards on three attempts. Jalen McCleod alertly picked up a fumble by LaJohntay Wester on a kickoff return when Wester was initially ruled down.

Coen and the players refused to let this become a trap game, despite all signs pointing in that direction. They were coming off a rout of the Colts, going back on the road, on a short week against a Ravens team that had every reason to fight like wounded animals.

And it was a clean game for the most part. There were only four penalties for 24 yards (three on Pregnon) and the Jags defense responded to the only turnover of the game by the offense when they forced a turnover on downs.

The Jags are 6-2 with 10 days before their next game. That's one game better than they were through eight games last season and they now have a four-game lead in the win column in the AFC South, with a November schedule of the hapless Titans twice and the New York Giants.

This article originally appeared on Florida Times-Union: Jaguars-Ravens report card shows a workmanlike effort in all categories

#2755. Brandon Aiyuk to return to 49ers' facility Friday afternoon, reportedly discussing forfeiture of $13 million bonus

Wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk's attempt at an NFL comeback is underway — though it's not entirely clear if or when he'll be able to pull it off.

More than two years after his last NFL snap, Aiyuk is pushing to make his way back, starting by hiring agent Drew Rosenhaus this week. Next up will be trying to make nice with the San Francisco 49ers, resolving a yearlong saga in order to let Aiyuk return to the field.

Rosenhaus told ESPN's Adam Schefter that he and Aiyuk will meet with the team at the 49ers' facility at 3 p.m. on Friday. "We will put our best foot forward and go from there," Rosenhaus told Schefter.

A potential wrinkle in that plan, though, is that Aiyuk owes the team around $13 million in bonus forfeiture as a result of the saga, per The Ringer's Tom Pelissero. The NFL Management Council and NFL Players Association are involved in trying to find a resolution, which could include an agreement to pay the money back, or potential arbitration, per Pelissero.

Aiyuk missed the entire 2025 season with ACL and MCL tears sustained in October 2024. But the real drama began last November, as the 49ers voided the receiver's 2026 contract guarantees after Aiyuk's relationship with the team became increasingly strained when he failed to show up for team rehab sessions and couldn't be reached for contact. In January, San Francisco general manager John Lynch said that Aiyuk had played his last snap with the Niners.

In June, Aiyuk continued to publicly ask the team to cut him so he could sign somewhere else, specifically the Washington Commanders. But that wasn't going to be possible until he was reinstated from the the reserve/left squad, where he landed because he failed to show up for mandatory team activities, including training camp.

In a video, the receiver said that he would never step foot in the 49ers' facility again; he also claimed that he had fired his agent.

Now, with Rosenhaus leading Aiyuk's negotiations, the receiver will hope to finally get his new start elsewhere, assuming the 49ers trade or cut him.

#2754. Cavs reportedly sign Norchad Omier to two-way deal; waive Riley Minix

The Cleveland Cavaliers have signed G League standout Norchad Omier to a two-way deal. The organization has released 26-year-old wing Riley Minix in order to do so.

Omier previously had agreed to join the Minnesota Timberwolves but has reportedly changed his mind and plans on signing with the Cavaliers instead.

Cleveland's developmental league has done a fine job in recent years. Sam Merrill, Craig Porter Jr. and Nae'Qwan Tomlin are recent examples of players who have climbed the ranks to sign standard contracts and play rotational minutes in the NBA.

Omier will look to follow the same path. He joins Cleveland's other two-way prospects, Ernest Udeh Jr. and Tristan Enaruna.

Minix, who signed a two-way deal with the Cavs in February of this year. The lanky forward never found his outside shot in limited opportunities with Cleveland.

The Cavs have a need for someone like Omier. Now more than ever. Bulky forwards don't grow on trees, apparently, and the Cavs would be thrilled to have another player rapidly graduate from the G League to the NBA this season.

All of this is easier said than done. Omier is not a polished player and isn't a threat to shoot from the perimeter. The 6'7" forward shot 20% from deep in 11 games with the Charge last season. But he has the size and athleticism to fill a momentary need for the Cavaliers — who are dealing with various injuries to a roster that already lacked wing depth.

Omier will have an opportunity now to continue proving himself in the G League, with a chance of earning minutes at the NBA level. This is what every prospect in his position is striving for. It's clear that he likes his chances in Cleveland, considering he backed out of a commitment with Minnesota to be here. The same can be said for the Cavs, who swiftly opened a two-way spot to get him.

The Cavs play two games at home this weekend. First, against the Philadelphia 76ers on Friday. Then, the New York Knicks on Saturday. We'll see if any of the two-way guys get a chance to play.
